). The other equalities follow from the inequalities of the previous paragraph. We also have:

d^{\prime}<b^{\prime}\Longleftrightarrow\varepsilon=-1\Longleftrightarrow c^{\prime}<b^{\prime}

as a result, by
corollary

2.20
, we have
c^{\prime}\equiv_{B}d^{\prime}
, concluding the proof.
∎

Remark 2.42
.

We recall some facts from previous subsections. Let
c=(c_{ij})_{ij}
be a finite
\textup{val}^{2}_{B}
-block of
B
-ramified points from
M
that is
\textup{val}^{3}_{B}
-separated, whose
\textup{val}^{3}_{B}
-blocks are the
(c_{i})_{i}=((c_{ij})_{j})_{i}
. Let
(p_{i})_{i}
be a weak
\textup{val}^{3}_{B}
-block extension of
c
. By
corollary

2.25
, and by separatedness, there exists
G
(resp.
H
) a unique
A
-type-definable(resp.
A
-
\vee
-definable) convex subgroup such that
H<G
, and exactly one of the following conditions holds for each
i
:

(1)

For every non-zero
f\in\textup{LC}^{|c_{i}|}(\mathbb{Q})
, there exists
b\in B
such that we have
p_{i}(x_{i})\models f(x_{i})-b\in G\setminus{\underline{G}_{M}}
.

(2)

For every non-zero
f\in\textup{LC}^{|c_{i}|}(\mathbb{Q})
, there exists
b\in B
such that we have
p_{i}(x_{i})\models f(x_{i})-b\in{\overline{H}^{M}}\setminus H
.

Of course we know exactly what
G
and
H
are, but this abstraction will simplify the definitions and proofs.

The following notion will mostly be used to understand strong
\textup{val}^{3}_{B}
-block extensions, and to prove
(C5)
:

Definition 2.43
.

If the first condition of
remark

2.42
holds, then we say that
p_{i}
is
outer
, else
p_{i}
is
inner
.

G
\underline{G}_{M}
\overline{H}^{M}
H
outer types
inner types

Lemma 2.44
.

Let
c=(c_{ij})_{ij}
be a finite
\textup{val}^{2}_{B}
-block of
B
-ramified points from
M
that is
\textup{val}^{3}_{B}
-separated, such that its
\textup{val}^{3}_{B}
-blocks are the
(c_{i})_{i}=((c_{ij})_{j})_{i}
. Suppose the indices
i
are ordered such that:
i<k\Longleftrightarrow\delta(c_{i}/B)<\delta(c_{k}/B)
. Let
(p_{i})_{i}
be a weak
\textup{val}^{3}_{B}
-block extension of
c
. Let
I
(resp.
O
) be the set of indices
i
such that
p_{i}
is inner (resp. outer). Then the following are equivalent:

•

(p_{i})_{i}
is a strong
\textup{val}^{3}_{B}
-block extension of
c
.

•

For every
i\in I,o\in O
, we have
i<o
.

Proof.

The realizations of
\bigcup\limits_{i}p_{i}
satisfy the first condition of
proposition

2.41
, so we are trying to characterize when some of them satisfy the second condition. Let
H=H(c/A)<G(c/A)=G
be the convex subgroups witnessing
remark

2.42
.

Suppose we have
i\in I
and
o\in O
such that
o<i
. Let
d\models\bigcup\limits_{j}p_{j}
. Then, as
p_{i}
is inner, we have
\delta(d_{i}/M)\in\Delta({\overline{H}^{M}})
. By the same argument, we have
\delta(d_{o}/M)\not\in\Delta({\underline{G}_{M}})
, therefore
\delta(d_{i}/M)<\delta(d_{o}/M)
, thus
d\not\equiv_{B}c
, and we proved the top-to-bottom implication.

Conversely, suppose we have
i<o
for every
i\in I
,
o\in O
. In order to satisfy the second condition of
proposition

2.41
, it suffices to prove that the following partial type is consistent:

q(x)=\bigcup\limits_{i}p_{i}(x_{i})\cup\left\{|x_{i,f(i)}-b_{i}|<\dfrac{1}{n}|x_{k,f(k)}-b_{k}|\ :\ i<k,n>0\right\}

with
f(i)
an arbitrary choice of a coordinate of
(c_{ij})_{j}
for each
i
, and with
b_{i}\in\textup{ram}(c_{i,f(i)}/B)
. Let us build a realization
\beta
of
q
.

Let
(\alpha_{i})_{i\in I}\models\bigcup\limits_{i\in I}p_{i}
. We define by induction, for each
i\in I
, a tuple
\beta_{i}\models p_{i}
such that, for
i<k
in
I
, we have
\Delta(\beta_{if(i)}-b_{i})<\Delta(\beta_{kf(k)}-b_{k})
. Let
N
be an
|M|^{+}
-saturated, strongly
|M|^{+}
-homogeneous elementary extension of
M
containing
(\alpha_{i})_{i\in I}
. Let
k\in I
, and suppose we defined
\beta_{i}\in N
for every
i<k
. By saturation, let
\gamma\in N
such that
\gamma\in{\overline{H}^{M}}\setminus H
, and
\Delta(\gamma)>\Delta(\beta_{if(i)}-b_{i})
for all
i<k
, i.e.
\gamma\in{\overline{H}^{M}}\setminus{\underline{({\overline{H}^{M}})}_{M+\sum\limits_{i<k}\mathbb{Q}\beta_{i,f(i)}}}
. Such a
\gamma
exists, as we are only dealing with indices of inner types. Then, by
corollary

2.20
, there exists
\varepsilon\in\{\pm 1\}
such that
b_{k}+\varepsilon\gamma\equiv_{M}\alpha_{k,f(k)}
. By strong homogeneity, there exists
\sigma\in\textup{Aut}(N/M)
such that
\sigma(\alpha_{k,f(k)})=b_{k}+\varepsilon\gamma
. We set
\beta_{k}=\sigma(\alpha_{k})
.

We can similarly define
(\beta_{o})_{o\in O}
. Then
(\beta_{i})_{i\in I\cup O}
satisfies
q
, concluding the proof.∎

Now we can prove
(C5)
:

Proposition 2.45
.

Let
c=(c_{ij})_{ij}
be a finite
\textup{val}^{2}_{B}
-block of
B
-ramified points from
M
that is
\textup{val}^{3}_{B}
-separated, whose
\textup{val}^{3}_{B}
-blocks are the
(c_{i})_{i}=((c_{ij})_{j})_{i}
. Let
(p_{i})_{i}
be a strong
\textup{val}^{3}_{B}
-block extension of
c
. Then some tensor product of the
p_{i}
is consistent with
\textup{tp}(c/B)
.

We will see much later, in
lemma

3.25
, that the tensor product in the following proof is in fact the only completion of
(p_{i})_{i}
(be it
\textup{Aut}(M/A)
-invariant or not) which is consistent with
\textup{tp}(c/B)
.

Proof.

Suppose the indices
i
are ordered such that
i<k
if and only if we have
\delta(c_{i}/B)<\delta(c_{k}/B)
. Define
I
,
O
,
G
,
H
, just as in
lemma

2.44
. Let
\alpha_{I}\models p_{I}=\bigcup\limits_{i\in I}p_{i}
,
\alpha_{O}\models p_{O}=\bigcup\limits_{o\in O}p_{o}
. As the conditions of
lemma

2.44
are satisfied, if we had
\alpha_{I}\equiv_{B}(c_{i})_{i\in I}
and
\alpha_{O}\equiv_{B}(c_{o})_{o\in O}
, then by
proposition

2.41
we would clearly have
\alpha_{I}\alpha_{O}\equiv_{B}c
. Moreover:

G(\alpha_{I}/M)={\overline{H}^{M}}<{\underline{G}_{M}}=H(\alpha_{O}/M)

thus one clearly sees that
\alpha_{I}
and
\alpha_{O}
are
\textup{val}^{2}_{M}
-blocks of distinct values, thus their types over
M
are weakly orthogonal by
proposition

2.36
. Therefore, if the respective types over
M
of
\alpha_{I}
and
\alpha_{O}
are tensor products of the
p_{i}
, then so is the type of
\alpha_{I}\alpha_{O}
. As a result, we can assume that either
I
or
O
is empty.

Suppose
O=\emptyset
. For
k\in I
, and
c^{\prime}=(c_{i})_{i>k}
, let us show that
c_{k}\models p_{k|D}
, with
D
the
\mathbb{Q}
-vector subspace generated by
Bc^{\prime}
. This implies that
\textup{tp}(c/B)
is consistent with the tensor product of the
p_{i}
in increasing order. Let
f
be non-zero in
\textup{LC}^{|c_{k}|}(\mathbb{Q})
. By
\textup{val}^{3}_{B}
-separatedness, we have
\delta(f(c_{k})/B)=\delta(c_{k}/B)
(call this Archimedean class
\delta
). Let
b
be in
\textup{ram}(f(c_{k})/B)
. As
i\in I
, we have
p_{k}(x_{k})\models f(x_{k})-b\in{\overline{H}^{M}}\setminus H
. It is enough to show that
f(c_{k})-b\in{\overline{H}^{D}}
, for the cut over
M
corresponding to the pushforward of
p_{k}
by
f
would be included in
\textup{ct}(f(c_{k})/D)
, and we could conclude. For
i>k
, let
\delta_{i}=\delta(c_{i}/B)
. By
\textup{val}^{3}_{B}
-separatedness, we clearly have
\Delta(D)=\Delta(B)\cup\left\{\delta_{i}|i>k\right\}
. By definition of
I
, we have
\delta<\delta_{i}
for every
i>k
. Moreover, we have by
remark

2.16
:

\textup{ct}(\delta_{i}/\Delta(B))=\textup{ct}_{>}(\Delta(H)\cap\Delta(B)/\Delta(B))

as a result,
\Delta(H)\cap\Delta(D)=\Delta(H)\cap\Delta(B)
, and:

\textup{ct}(\delta/\Delta(D))=\textup{ct}_{>}(\Delta(H)\cap\Delta(D)/\Delta(D))

thus
\delta\in\Delta({\overline{H}^{D}})
, concluding the proof.

\Delta(0)
\delta
\delta_{k+1}
\delta_{k+2}
\ldots
\Delta(H)
\Delta(\overline{H}^{D})
\Delta(\overline{H}^{B})

Now, if instead
I=\emptyset
, then a similar proof would show that the tensor product of the
p_{o}
in decreasing order would be consistent with
\textup{tp}(c/B)
.∎

Corollary 2.46
.

The property
(C5)
holds.

Proof.

Proposition
2.45
deals with the particular case of a
\textup{val}^{2}_{B}
-block. Then, given the global invariant extensions of the types of each
\textup{val}^{2}_{B}
-block given by
proposition

2.45
, some tensor product of those global types witnesses the statement, by
proposition

2.37
and
proposition

2.32
.
∎

3.
How to build a block extension

In the previous section, we defined the key valuations
\textup{val}^{i}_{A}
, and we proved the properties
(C5)
,
(C6)
,
(C7)
. In this section, we give a proof of
(C4)
. The main technical goal is to show that
\underset{A}{\downfree^{\mathbf{cut}}}B
coincides with
\underset{A}{\downfree^{\mathbf{inv}}}B
, when what we put on the left is a
\textup{val}^{3}_{B}
-separated
\textup{val}^{3}_{B}
-block. This will show the existence of a weak block extension, and then it will not be hard to show that such a block extension can be chosen strong. In fact, given some
\textup{val}^{3}_{B}
-separated tuple
c
, we describe explicitly the homeomorphism type of the space of global
\textup{Aut}(M/A)
-invariant extensions of
\textup{tp}(c/B)
.
We adopt in this section
1.27
.

3.1.
The Archimedean case

In this subsection, we deal with the case where
c
is a
\textup{val}^{3}_{B}
-separated block of
B
-Archimedean points from
M
.

Remark 3.1
.

Suppose
c
is a
\textup{val}^{3}_{B}
-separated block of
B
-Archimedean points from
M
. As
c\underset{A}{\downfree^{\mathbf{cut}}}B
, the
c_{i}
must be Archimedean over
A
. Indeed, by contraposition, if
c_{i}
is ramified over
A
for some
i
, then we cannot have
\delta(c_{i}/A)\in\Delta(B)
, otherwise there would exist
b\in B
,
a\in A
such that
\delta(c_{i}/A)=\Delta(c_{i}-a)=\Delta(b)
, thus
c_{i}
would lie between
a+\dfrac{1}{n}b
and
a+nb
for some
n\in\mathbb{Z}
, which would contradict
c\underset{A}{\downfree^{\mathbf{cut}}}B
. Now, as
\Delta(c_{i}-a)\not\in\Delta(B)
we have a contradiction with the hypothesis that all the
c_{i}
are Archimedean over
B
.

Assumptions 3.2
.

On top of
1.27
, we assume that
c
is a
\textup{val}^{3}_{B}
-separated block of
B
-Archimedean points from
M
. By
remark

3.1
and
lemma

2.23
, let us also fix
G=G(c/A)=G(c/B)
.

Lemma 3.3
.

Let
p_{i}\in S(M)
be a global
\textup{Aut}(M/A)
-invariant extension of
\textup{tp}(c_{i}/B)
. Suppose
q
is a complete global extension of
\textup{tp}(c/B)
which extends
\bigcup\limits_{i}p_{i}
. If we have
q(x)\models f(x)-f(c)\not\in{\underline{G}_{M}}
for all non-zero
f\in\textup{LC}^{n}(\mathbb{Q})
, then
q
is
\textup{Aut}(M/A)
-invariant.

Proof.

Suppose by contradiction
q
is not
\textup{Aut}(M/A)
-invariant. Then there exists a non-zero
f\in\textup{LC}^{n}(\mathbb{Q})
, and a cut
X
over
M
, such that
q(x)\models f(x)\in X
, and the global
1
-type induced by
X
is not
\textup{Aut}(M/A)
-invariant. As
c\underset{A}{\downfree^{\mathbf{cut}}}B
, we have
f(c)\underset{A}{\downfree^{\mathbf{inv}}}B
. By
lemma

2.23
, we clearly see that the two global
\textup{Aut}(M/A)
-invariant extensions of
\textup{tp}(f(c)/B)
correspond to the translates by
f(c)
of the two connected components of
G\setminus{\underline{G}_{M}}
. We obtain a contradiction by showing that
X
coincides with one of those. It is enough to show that
q(x)\models f(x)-f(c)\in G\setminus{\underline{G}_{M}}
. By hypothesis, we just need to have
q(x)\models f(x)-f(c)\in G
, which follows from the fact that
c
is a
\textup{val}^{3}_{B}
-separated block. Indeed,
\textup{val}^{3}_{B}(f(c))=\textup{val}^{3}_{B}(c)
, therefore
f(c)
is Archimedean over
B
, and
G(f(c)/B)=G
. This implies by
proposition

2.18
that
\textup{tp}(f(c)/B)\models y-f(c)\in G
, concluding the proof.
∎

It turns out that the space of global
\textup{Aut}(M/A)
-invariant extensions of
\textup{tp}(c/B)
in the Archimedean case has a very simple description:

Proposition 3.4
.

Let
N
be some
|M|^{+}
-saturated and strongly
|M|^{+}
-homogeneous elementary extension of
M
. Let
H=({\underline{G}_{M}})({N})
be the convex subgroup of
{N}
generated by
G(M)
, and let
{N}^{\prime}
be the ordered Abelian group
{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.41666pt}{$\displaystyle{{N}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.41666pt}{$\displaystyle{H}$}}{\raisebox{3.41666pt}{$\textstyle{{N}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.41666pt}{$\textstyle{H}$}}{\raisebox{2.39166pt}{$\scriptstyle{{N}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.39166pt}{$\scriptstyle{H}$}}{\raisebox{1.70833pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{{N}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.70833pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{H}$}}}
, which is a model of
DOAG
by saturation. Let
F_{1}\subseteq S^{|c|}(M)
be the closed set of
\textup{Aut}(M/A)
-invariant extensions of
\textup{tp}(c/B)
, and
F_{2}\subseteq S^{|c|}(\{0\})
be the closed set of types of
\mathbb{Q}
-free families. Then the map:

g:\ \textup{tp}^{{N}}(\alpha/M)\longmapsto\textup{tp}^{{N}^{\prime}}((\alpha_{i}-c_{i}\textup{ mod }H)_{i}/\{0\})

is a well-defined homeomorphism
F_{1}\longrightarrow F_{2}
.

Proof.

Let
f\in\textup{LC}^{n}(\mathbb{Q})
be non-zero, and let
\alpha\in N
realize a global
\textup{Aut}(M/A)
-invariant extension of
\textup{tp}(c/B)
. Then, by separatedness,
f(c)
is Archimedean over
A
, and
G(f(c)/A)=G
, thus
f(c)-f(\alpha)\not\in{\underline{G}_{M}}
by
lemma

2.23
, i.e.
f(c)-f(\alpha)\not\in H
. No non-trivial linear combination of
(c_{i}-\alpha_{i})_{i}
belongs to
H
, hence
(c_{i}-\alpha_{i}\textup{ mod }H)_{i}
is
\mathbb{Q}
-free.

Moreover, if
\beta\equiv_{M}\alpha
, then we have
f((\beta_{i}-c_{i}\textup{ mod }H)_{i})>0
in
N^{\prime}
if and only if
f((\beta_{i}-c_{i})_{i})\in\bigcap\limits_{m\in H}]m,+\infty[
in
N
, if and only if the same holds for
\alpha
, therefore
(\beta_{i}-c_{i}\textup{ mod }H)_{i}\equiv_{\{0\}}(\alpha_{i}-c_{i}\textup{ mod }H)_{i}
in
N^{\prime}
. It follows that
g
is well-defined, and it is continuous by definition (and quantifier elimination).

Let
p_{1},p_{2}\in F_{1}
be distinct. Then there exists two distinct cuts
X_{1},X_{2}
over
M
, and
f\in\textup{LC}^{n}(\mathbb{Q})
non-zero, such that
p_{i}(x)\models f(x)\in X_{i}
. However, by
lemma

2.23
,
X_{i}
must be the translate by
f(c)
of a connected component of
G\setminus{\underline{G}_{M}}
. As a result, one of the
X_{i}
(say,
X_{1}
) is the leftmost connected component, and the other is the rightmost one. We have:

p_{1}(x)\models f(x)-f(c)<{\underline{G}_{M}}

thus
g(p_{1})\models f(x)<0
. With the same reasoning,
g(p_{2})\models f(x)>0
, and we conclude that
g(p_{1})\neq g(p_{2})
, proving that
g
is injective.

Now let us prove surjectivity. Let
\beta^{\prime}
be a
\mathbb{Q}
-free family from
{N}^{\prime}
. Let
\beta
be a preimage of
\beta^{\prime}
by
\pi:\ {N}\longrightarrow{N}^{\prime}
. Let
d_{1}\in{N}
such that
\Delta(d_{1})>\Delta(\beta_{i})
for every
i
. Let
d_{2}\in{N}
such that
\Delta(d_{2})\in\Delta(G)\setminus\Delta({\underline{G}_{M}})
. Then
|d_{1}|
and
|d_{2}|
have the same cut over
G(M)
:
+\infty
, the cut of positive elements larger than all the elements of
G(M)
. By strong homogeneity, there is an automorphism
\sigma\in\textup{Aut}({N}/G(M))
such that
\sigma(|d_{1}|)=|d_{2}|
. Since
\sigma
fixes
G(M)
, we have
\sigma(H)=H
, thus
\sigma^{\prime}:\ \pi(x)\longmapsto\pi(\sigma(x))
is a well-defined automorphism of the ordered group
{N}^{\prime}
. As
\beta^{\prime}
is
\mathbb{Q}
-free, so is
\sigma^{\prime}(\beta^{\prime})
. As
\sigma(\beta)
is a preimage of
\sigma^{\prime}(\beta^{\prime})
, none of its elements is in
H
. Moreover,
|\sigma(\beta_{i})|\leqslant\sigma(|d_{1}|)=|d_{2}|\in G
for every
i
, thus
\sigma(\beta_{i})\in G
. As
\textup{tp}^{N^{\prime}}(\beta^{\prime}/\{0\})=\textup{tp}^{N^{\prime}}(\sigma^{\prime}(\beta^{\prime})/\{0\})
, and we are looking for a preimage of this type by
g
, we may assume
\sigma=id
, and thus, by
\mathbb{Q}
-freeness of
\beta^{\prime}
,
f(\beta)\in G\setminus{\underline{G}_{M}}
for every non-zero
f\in\textup{LC}^{n}(\mathbb{Q})
. Let
p=\textup{tp}((c_{i}+\beta_{i})_{i}/M)
. As
\beta_{i}\in G
for all
i
,
p
extends
\textup{tp}(c/B)
, and it is
\textup{Aut}(M/A)
-invariant by
lemma

3.3
. Thus
p\in F_{1}
, and it is a preimage of
\textup{tp}(\beta^{\prime}/\{0\})
by
g
.

We showed that
g
is a continuous bijection, and we conclude by compactness and separation.
∎

Since
F_{2}
is non-empty, we have in particular proved the following:

Corollary 3.5
.

If
c
is a
\textup{val}^{3}_{B}
-separated block of
B
-Archimedean points from
M
, then we have
c\underset{A}{\downfree^{\mathbf{inv}}}B
.

The next subsection gives tools to understand what
F_{2}
looks like. For the most part, it is used to deal with the ramified blocks.

3.2.
Archimedean groups

We need to define some algebraic notions about Archimedean groups in order to well-understand the global invariant extensions of the types of ramified blocks. Let us start with well-known facts:

Proposition 3.6
.

Let
G
be an ordered Abelian group. Then
G
has at most two Archimedean classes (including
\Delta(0)
) if and only if it embeds into
(\mathbb{R},+,<)
. In that case, for all
x\in G_{>0}
, for all
\mu\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}
, there exists a unique embedding of ordered groups
\sigma:G\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}
sending
x
to
\mu
.

The idea of the proof is to send each
y\in G
to
\mu\cdot\left(\sup\left\{\lambda\in\mathbb{Q}|\lambda y<x\right\}\right)^{-1}
.

Remark 3.7
.

In the special case
G=\mathbb{R}
, we see that an embedding of ordered groups
\sigma:\ \mathbb{R}\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}
is uniquely determined by the choice of
\sigma(1)
, hence
\sigma
actually coincides with the automorphism
x\longmapsto\sigma(1)\cdot x
. This establishes an easy description of the group of ordered group automorphisms of
\mathbb{R}
, which is naturally isomorphic to the multiplicative group
\mathbb{R}_{>0}
.

Definition 3.8
.

We define
\mathbb{P}^{+}_{n}
, the space of half-lines in dimension
n
(plus the origin) as the set of orbits of
\mathbb{R}^{n}
under the automorphisms of the ordered group
\mathbb{R}
. We call
\mathbb{P}^{+}
the canonical map
\mathbb{R}^{n}\longrightarrow\mathbb{P}^{+}_{n}
.

Suppose
G
is an Archimedean ordered Abelian group, and let
u
be a finite tuple from
G
. By the universal property of
proposition

3.6
, we see that
\mathbb{P}^{+}(\sigma(u))
is always the same for any ordered group embedding
\sigma:\ G\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}
. As a result, we can define
\mathbb{P}^{+}(u)\in\mathbb{P}^{+}_{|u|}
as this unique class.

Remark 3.9
.

If
f\in\textup{LC}^{n}(\mathbb{Q})
, then
f
commutes with any automorphism, thus one can easily show that
f(\mathbb{P}^{+}(u))=\mathbb{P}^{+}(f(u))\in\mathbb{P}^{+}_{1}
for any
u\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
. However,
\mathbb{P}^{+}_{1}
is not very complicated to describe:
f(\mathbb{P}^{+}(u))
is either
\mathbb{R}_{>0}
,
\mathbb{R}_{<0}
or
\{0\}
.

Definition 3.10
.

Let
u\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
. We say that
\mathbb{P}^{+}(u)
is
\mathbb{Q}
-free
if
u
is
\mathbb{Q}
-free. By
remark

3.9
, this definition does not depend on the choice of the representative
u
.

Proposition 3.11
.

Let
G
be an ordered Abelian group. Then for any subgroup
H
of
G
, and
\Delta(0)\neq\delta\in\Delta(G)
, the quotient:

H_{\delta}={\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.41666pt}{$\displaystyle{H_{\leqslant\delta}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.41666pt}{$\displaystyle{H_{<\delta}}$}}{\raisebox{3.41666pt}{$\textstyle{H_{\leqslant\delta}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.41666pt}{$\textstyle{H_{<\delta}}$}}{\raisebox{2.39166pt}{$\scriptstyle{H_{\leqslant\delta}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.39166pt}{$\scriptstyle{H_{<\delta}}$}}{\raisebox{1.70833pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{H_{\leqslant\delta}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.70833pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{H_{<\delta}}$}}}

as defined in
definition

1.26
, is Archimedean.

In the literature, the
(H_{\delta})_{\delta\in\Delta(H)}
are called the “components” of
H
.

Definition 3.12
.

Let
\delta\in\Delta(M)
, and
u
a tuple from
M_{=\delta}
. By
proposition

3.11
, we can define
\mathbb{P}^{+}(u)=\mathbb{P}^{+}(u\textup{ mod }M_{<\delta})
with respect to the definition
3.8
in the Archimedean group
{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.41666pt}{$\displaystyle{M_{\leqslant\delta}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.41666pt}{$\displaystyle{M_{<\delta}}$}}{\raisebox{3.41666pt}{$\textstyle{M_{\leqslant\delta}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.41666pt}{$\textstyle{M_{<\delta}}$}}{\raisebox{2.39166pt}{$\scriptstyle{M_{\leqslant\delta}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.39166pt}{$\scriptstyle{M_{<\delta}}$}}{\raisebox{1.70833pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{M_{\leqslant\delta}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.70833pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{M_{<\delta}}$}}}
.

Suppose
d=(d_{1}\ldots d_{n})
is a
\textup{val}^{3}_{B}
-block of
B
-ramified elements of
M
, and let
\delta=\delta(d_{i}/B)\in\Delta(M)
. Let
b_{i}\in\textup{ram}(d_{i}/B)
. Then we can define
\mathbb{P}^{+}(d/B)=\mathbb{P}^{+}((d_{i}-b_{i})_{i})
. As
H(d_{i}/B)\leqslant M_{<\delta}
, by
remark

2.12
, this definition does not depend on the choice of the
b_{i}
.

Proposition 3.13
.

Let
d=(d_{1}\ldots d_{n})
be a finite
\textup{val}^{3}_{B}
-block of
B
-ramified points from
M
. Then
d
is
\textup{val}^{3}_{B}
-separated if and only if
\mathbb{P}^{+}(d/B)
is
\mathbb{Q}
-free.

Proof.

Let
b_{i}\in\textup{ram}(d_{i}/B)
, and let
\delta=\Delta(d/B)
.

Then
d
is not
\textup{val}^{3}_{B}
-separated if and only if
\textup{val}^{3}_{B}(f(d))<\textup{val}^{3}_{B}(d)
for some non-zero
f\in\textup{LC}^{n}(\mathbb{Q})
, if and only if there exists such an
f
with
\Delta(f(d)-f(b))<\delta
, if and only if we have
f
such that
\{0\}=f(\mathbb{P}^{+}((d_{i}-b_{i})_{i}))=f(\mathbb{P}^{+}(d/B))
, if and only if
\mathbb{P}^{+}(d/B)
is not
\mathbb{Q}
-free.
∎

3.3.
The ramified case

Assumptions 3.14
.

In this subsection, on top of
1.27
suppose
c=(c_{i})_{i}
is a
\textup{val}^{3}_{B}
-separated
\textup{val}^{3}_{B}
-block of
B
-ramified points. Fix
G=G(c/B)
,
H=H(c/B)
,
\delta=\delta(c/B)
. For each
i
, let
b_{i}
be in
\textup{ram}(c_{i}/B)
, and write
b=(b_{i})_{i}
.

Recall that
corollary

2.20
characterizes the type of a ramified singleton in terms of cuts of Archimedean classes. We can now extend this characterization to the type of our
\textup{val}^{3}_{B}
-separated ramified
\textup{val}^{3}_{B}
-block:

Proposition 3.15
.

Let
d=(d_{i})_{i}
be a tuple from
M
. Then we have
d\equiv_{B}c
if and only if the following conditions hold:

•

d_{i}\equiv_{B}c_{i}
for every
i
.

•

d
is a
\textup{val}^{3}_{B}
-block, i.e.
\delta(d_{i}/B)=\delta(d_{j}/B)
for every
i
,
j
.

•

\mathbb{P}^{+}(d/B)=\mathbb{P}^{+}(c/B)
.

Proof.

Suppose the conditions of the list hold. Let
f\in\textup{LC}^{n}(\mathbb{Q})
be non-zero. Then, as
c
is
\textup{val}^{3}_{B}
-separated,
\mathbb{P}^{+}(c/B)
is
\mathbb{Q}
-free, therefore
\Delta(f(c)-f(b))=\delta
. As a result,
\textup{ct}(f(c)/B)
is the translate by
f(b)
of one of the connected components of
G\setminus H
. The same holds for
d
, but we need to make sure that
f(c)
and
f(d)
lie in the same connected component. The thing is,
f(c)
lies in the rightmost one if and only if
(f(c)-f(b)\textup{ mod }M_{<\delta})>0
in the ordered group
{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.41666pt}{$\displaystyle{M_{\leqslant\delta}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.41666pt}{$\displaystyle{M_{<\delta}}$}}{\raisebox{3.41666pt}{$\textstyle{M_{\leqslant\delta}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.41666pt}{$\textstyle{M_{<\delta}}$}}{\raisebox{2.39166pt}{$\scriptstyle{M_{\leqslant\delta}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.39166pt}{$\scriptstyle{M_{<\delta}}$}}{\raisebox{1.70833pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{M_{\leqslant\delta}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.70833pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{M_{<\delta}}$}}}
, if and only if
\mathbb{P}^{+}(f(c)-f(b))=\mathbb{R}_{>0}
, if and only if
\mathbb{R}_{>0}=f(\mathbb{P}^{+}((c_{i}-b_{i})_{i}))=f(\mathbb{P}^{+}(c/B))
. Now, by the hypothesis
\mathbb{P}^{+}(d/B)=\mathbb{P}^{+}(c/B)
, those conditions are equivalent to
f(d)
lying in the rightmost connected component, and we get the bottom-to-top direction.

Conversely, suppose now
d\equiv_{B}c
, in particular the first condition holds. As for the second,
d
must be a
\textup{val}^{3}_{B}
-block, otherwise it would be witnessed in its type by formulas
(|x_{i}-b_{i}|>n|x_{j}-b_{j}|)_{n}
for some
i\neq j
, which would contradict the hypothesis on
c
. By strong homogeneity of
M
, let
\sigma\in\textup{Aut}(M/B)
be such that
\sigma(c)=d
. The equivalence relation
\Delta(x)=\Delta(y)
is an
\vee
-definable subset of
S^{2}(\emptyset)
, therefore it is invariant by
\sigma
. As a result, the map:

\sigma^{\prime}:\ (x\textup{ mod }M_{<\delta})\longmapsto(\sigma(x)\textup{ mod }M_{<\delta(d/B)})

is a well-defined automorphism of ordered groups from the quotient
{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.41666pt}{$\displaystyle{M_{\leqslant\delta}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.41666pt}{$\displaystyle{M_{<\delta}}$}}{\raisebox{3.41666pt}{$\textstyle{M_{\leqslant\delta}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.41666pt}{$\textstyle{M_{<\delta}}$}}{\raisebox{2.39166pt}{$\scriptstyle{M_{\leqslant\delta}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.39166pt}{$\scriptstyle{M_{<\delta}}$}}{\raisebox{1.70833pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{M_{\leqslant\delta}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.70833pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{M_{<\delta}}$}}}
to
{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.41666pt}{$\displaystyle{M_{\leqslant\delta(d/B)}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.41666pt}{$\displaystyle{M_{<\delta(d/B)}}$}}{\raisebox{3.41666pt}{$\textstyle{M_{\leqslant\delta(d/B)}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.41666pt}{$\textstyle{M_{<\delta(d/B)}}$}}{\raisebox{2.39166pt}{$\scriptstyle{M_{\leqslant\delta(d/B)}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.39166pt}{$\scriptstyle{M_{<\delta(d/B)}}$}}{\raisebox{1.70833pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{M_{\leqslant\delta(d/B)}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.70833pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{M_{<\delta(d/B)}}$}}}
, which sends each
c_{i}-b_{i}
to
d_{i}-b_{i}
. Let
\tau
be an embedding of ordered groups
{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.41666pt}{$\displaystyle{M_{\leqslant\delta(d/B)}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.41666pt}{$\displaystyle{M_{<\delta(d/B)}}$}}{\raisebox{3.41666pt}{$\textstyle{M_{\leqslant\delta(d/B)}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.41666pt}{$\textstyle{M_{<\delta(d/B)}}$}}{\raisebox{2.39166pt}{$\scriptstyle{M_{\leqslant\delta(d/B)}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.39166pt}{$\scriptstyle{M_{<\delta(d/B)}}$}}{\raisebox{1.70833pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{M_{\leqslant\delta(d/B)}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.70833pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{M_{<\delta(d/B)}}$}}}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
. Then
\mathbb{P}^{+}(c/B)\ni\tau\circ\sigma^{\prime}((c_{i}-b_{i})_{i})=\tau((d_{i}-b_{i})_{i})\in\mathbb{P}^{+}(d/B)
, thus the last condition of the list holds, concluding the proof.
∎

Lemma 3.16
.

Choose an arbitrary index
i
. Suppose that
p
is a global
\textup{Aut}(M/A)
-invariant extension of
\textup{tp}(c_{i}/B)
. Then
q=p\cup\textup{tp}(c/B)
is a complete global type in
S(M)
.

Note that
q
may not be
\textup{Aut}(M/A)
-invariant. We will see an example soon.

Proof.

Proving that
q
is consistent is easy: choose
d
a realization of
p
in some strongly
|B|^{+}
-homogeneous elementary extension
N
of
M
, choose
\sigma
in
\textup{Aut}(N/B)
such that
\sigma(c_{i})=d
, then
\sigma(c)
is a realization of
q
.

Let
\alpha\models q
, and
\delta^{\prime}=\Delta(\alpha_{i}-b_{i})
. As
\alpha_{i}\models p
, we have:

\delta^{\prime}\in(\Delta(G)\setminus\Delta({\underline{G}_{M}}))\cup(\Delta({\overline{H}^{M}})\setminus\Delta(H))

in particular it does not belong to
\Delta(M)
. For every
j\neq i
, not only do we have
\Delta(\alpha_{j}-b_{j})\not\in\Delta(B)
as
\alpha_{j}\equiv_{B}c_{j}
, but in fact we have
\Delta(\alpha_{j}-b_{j})=\delta^{\prime}
by the second condition of
proposition

3.15
. In particular
\alpha
is a
\textup{val}^{3}_{M}
-block, and we have:

\mathbb{P}^{+}(\alpha/M)=\mathbb{P}^{+}((\alpha_{j}-b_{j})_{j})=\mathbb{P}^{+}(\alpha/B)=\mathbb{P}^{+}(c/B)

The last equality follows from
proposition

3.15
, and the others follow from the definitions. We established that, for all
\beta\models q
,
\beta
is a
\textup{val}^{3}_{M}
-block, and
\mathbb{P}^{+}(\beta/M)=\mathbb{P}^{+}(\alpha/M)
. By
proposition

3.15
(with
c,B
replaced by
\alpha,M
), in order to check that
q
is complete, it is enough to show that for every
j
, the pushforward
q_{j}
of
q
by the
j
-th projection, is a complete type in
S(M)
.

Let
f\in\textup{LC}^{N}(\mathbb{Q})
be the projection on the
j
-th coordinate.

•

On one hand,
q_{j}(x_{j})\models x_{j}>b_{j}
if and only if
f(\mathbb{P}^{+}(c/B))>0
.

•

On the other hand, we have:

q(x)\models\textup{ct}(\Delta(x_{j}-b_{j})/\Delta(M))=\textup{ct}(\Delta(x_{i}-b_{i})/\Delta(M))=\textup{ct}(\delta^{\prime}/\Delta(M))

The first equality follows from the fact that the realizations of
q
are
\textup{val}^{3}_{M}
-blocks, and the last equality follows from
corollary

2.20
, as
p
is complete. In particular:

q_{j}(x_{j})\models\textup{ct}(\Delta(x_{j}-b_{j})/\Delta(M))=\textup{ct}(\delta^{\prime}/\Delta(M))

Then, we can apply
corollary

2.20
to show that
q_{j}
, and thus
q
, is complete.
∎

Proposition 3.17
.

Let
i
(if it exists) be such that
c_{i}
is Archimedean over
A
. Then
\textup{tp}(c_{i}/B)
has exactly one global
\textup{Aut}(M/A)
-invariant extension
p
, and
p\cup\textup{tp}(c/B)
is a complete,
\textup{Aut}(M/A)
-invariant global type in
S(M)
.

Proof.

Suppose for simplicity
c_{i}>b_{i}
(else apply the proposition with
c_{i},b_{i}
replaced by
-c_{i},-b_{i}
). Recall that
c\underset{A}{\downfree^{\mathbf{cut}}}B
, thus
\textup{tp}(c_{i}/B)
must have at least one global
\textup{Aut}(M/A)
-invariant extension. By
lemma

2.23
,
G(c_{i}/A)=G
, and the types
p_{>},p_{<}
over
M
corresponding respectively to the cuts:

\textup{ct}_{>}(\textup{ct}(c_{i}/A)/M)=\textup{ct}_{>}(b_{i}\textup{ mod }G(M)/M)

\textup{ct}_{<}(\textup{ct}(c_{i}/A)/M)=\textup{ct}_{<}(b_{i}\textup{ mod }G(M)/M)

are clearly global
\textup{Aut}(M/A)
-invariant extensions of
\textup{tp}(c_{i}/A)
. These two types are the only ones implying that
x-b_{i}\in G\setminus{\underline{G}_{M}}
. As
c_{i}>b_{i}
, only
p_{>}
extends
\textup{tp}(c_{i}/B)
. By
lemma

2.23
, there are no other global
\textup{Aut}(M/A)
-invariant extension of
\textup{tp}(c_{i}/B)
.

Let
q=p_{>}\cup\textup{tp}(c/B)
, which is complete by
lemma

3.16
. Let us show that
q
is
\textup{Aut}(M/A)
-invariant. As
\mathbb{P}^{+}(c/B)
is
\mathbb{Q}
-free, the realizations of
q
are
\textup{val}^{3}_{M}
-separated by
proposition

3.13
. As a result, for every non-zero
f\in\textup{LC}^{N}(\mathbb{Q})
,
q(x)\models f(x)-f(b)\in G\setminus{\underline{G}_{M}}
. As
G=G(c_{i}/A)
and
{\underline{G}_{M}}
are
\textup{Aut}(M/A)
-invariant, we only have to show that
X=f(b)\textup{ mod }{\underline{G}_{M}}
is
\textup{Aut}(M/A)
-invariant as a subset of
M
, because:

q(x)\models\textup{ct}(f(x)/M)\in\{\textup{ct}_{>}(X/M),\textup{ct}_{<}(X/M)\}

However, as a subset of
M
,
X
coincides with
f(b)\textup{ mod }G(M)
. If
f(c)
is Archimedean over
A
, then
X=\textup{ct}(f(c)/A)(M)
, which is invariant under
\textup{Aut}(M/A)
. Else, by definition, there exists
a\in A
such that
f(c)-a\in G(M)
. However, we also have
f(c)-f(b)\in G(M)
, therefore
X=a\textup{ mod }G(M)
, which is
\textup{Aut}(M/A)
-invariant. This concludes the proof.
∎

Note that
p_{>}
, and thus
q
, is outer (with respect to
definition

2.43
).

Remark 3.18
.

If
j\neq i
, and
c_{j}
is ramified over
A
, then
\textup{tp}(c_{j}/B)
might have two global
\textup{Aut}(M/A)
-invariant extensions, and the union of each of those extensions with
\textup{tp}(c/B)
would be a consistent, complete type in
S(M)
. However, one of the two will not be
\textup{Aut}(M/A)
-invariant, as it will not be consistent with
p_{>}
. In the above proof, we would encounter a problem where we would want to show that
X
is
\textup{Aut}(M/A)
-invariant, because we would have to deal with the case where
f(c)
is Archimedean over
A
, and
X=f(b)\textup{ mod }H
.

This is exactly what happens in the following example:

Example 3.19
.

Let
A=\mathbb{Q}
,
B=\mathbb{Q}+\mathbb{Q}\sqrt{2}
,
\varepsilon
a positive infinitesimal element,
c_{1}=\sqrt{2}+\varepsilon
, and
c_{2}=\varepsilon\cdot\sqrt{2}
. Then
c_{1}c_{2}\underset{A}{\downfree^{\mathbf{cut}}}B
, and
c_{1}c_{2}
is a
\textup{val}^{3}_{B}
-separated block. Moreover,
\textup{tp}(c_{1}/B)
has exactly one global
\textup{Aut}(M/A)
-invariant extension, and
\textup{tp}(c_{2}/B)
has two, one of which is
p
, the type of a positive element that is infinitesimal with respect to
M
. Then
q=p\cup\textup{tp}(c_{1}c_{2}/B)
is complete and consistent in
S(M)
, but it is not
\textup{Aut}(M/A)
-invariant. The reason is that
q(x,y)\models\sqrt{2}\leqslant x\leqslant\sqrt{2}+\varepsilon
.

Proposition 3.20
.

Suppose
c_{i}
is ramified over
A
for every
i
. Choose an arbitrary index
i
. Let
H^{\prime}=H(c/A),G^{\prime}=G(c/A)
. If
H\neq H^{\prime}
or
G\neq G^{\prime}
, then
\textup{tp}(c_{i}/B)
has exactly one global
\textup{Aut}(M/A)
-invariant extension, else it has exactly two. Moreover, if
p
is such an extension, then
p\cup\textup{tp}(c/B)
is a complete
\textup{Aut}(M/A)
-invariant type in
S(M)
.

Proof.

We can assume
b_{j}\in A
for every
j
. Suppose
c_{i}>b_{i}
. As
c\underset{A}{\downfree^{\mathbf{cut}}}B
, recall that
H^{\prime}\leqslant H\leqslant G\leqslant G^{\prime}
. Let
p_{<},p_{>}
be the global
1
-types corresponding to the respective cuts
\textup{ct}_{<}(\textup{ct}(c_{i}/A)/M)=\textup{ct}_{>}(b_{i}\textup{ mod }H^{\prime}/M)
,
\textup{ct}_{>}(\textup{ct}(c_{i}/A)/M)=\textup{ct}_{>}(b_{i}\textup{ mod }{\underline{G^{\prime}}_{M}}/M)
. One can see that these two types are the only global
\textup{Aut}(M/A)
-invariant extensions of
\textup{tp}(c_{i}/A)
. Moreover
p_{<}
(resp.
p_{>}
) is consistent with
\textup{tp}(c_{i}/B)
if and only if
H=H^{\prime}
(resp.
G=G^{\prime}
).

Now, let
p
be a global
\textup{Aut}(M/A)
-invariant extension of
\textup{tp}(c_{i}/B)
, and
q=p\cup\textup{tp}(c/B)
. Then
q
is complete by
lemma

3.16
. Let us show that
q
is
\textup{Aut}(M/A)
-invariant.

Let
f\in\textup{LC}^{n}(\mathbb{Q})
be non-zero. One just has to show that both the cosets
f(b)\textup{ mod }H^{\prime}(M)
and
f(b)\textup{ mod }G^{\prime}(M)
are
\textup{Aut}(M/A)
-invariant. However,
H^{\prime}(M)
and
G^{\prime}(M)
are both
\textup{Aut}(M/A)
-invariant, and the
b_{i}
were now chosen in
A
. This concludes the proof.
∎

Note that
p_{<}
(thus the extension
p_{<}\cup\textup{tp}(c/B)
) is inner, while
p_{>}
(thus
p_{>}\cup\textup{tp}(c/B)
) is outer.

Remark 3.21
.

The groups
H^{\prime}
and
G^{\prime}
have the same points in
A
. By quantifier elimination, it is not hard to show that
G^{\prime}
does not admit an
A
-(
\vee
/type)-definable proper convex subgroup that strictly contains
H^{\prime}
. Recall that
H^{\prime}\leqslant H\leqslant G\leqslant G^{\prime}
by cut-independence. As a result, if
G\neq G^{\prime}
, then
G
cannot be
A
-type-definable. Similarly, if
H\neq H^{\prime}
, then
H
is not
A
-
\vee
-definable.

Corollary 3.22
.

If one of the
c_{i}
is Archimedean over
A
, or
H
is not
A
-
\vee
-definable, or
G
is not
A
-type-definable, then
\textup{tp}(c/B)
has exactly one global
\textup{Aut}(M/A)
-invariant extension. Else, it has exactly two. More precisely, if
G
is
A
-type-definable, then
\textup{tp}(c/B)
admits a global
\textup{Aut}(M/A)
-invariant extension that is outer ; while if
H
is
A
-
\vee
-definable and none of the
c_{i}
is Archimedean over
A
, then
\textup{tp}(c/B)
has a global
\textup{Aut}(M/A)
-invariant extension that is inner.

3.4.
Gluing everything together

We recall that we adopt
1.27
. We now have enough tools to prove
(C4)
. We can actually prove the following more precise statement:

Proposition 3.23
.

Suppose
c=(c_{ik})_{ik}
is a finite
\textup{val}^{2}_{B}
-block of
B
-ramified points from
M
that is
\textup{val}^{3}_{B}
-separated, such that its
\textup{val}^{3}_{B}
-blocks are the
(c_{i})_{i\in E}=((c_{ik})_{k})_{i\in E}
. Let
G=G(c/B)
,
H=H(c/B)
. Suppose the indices
i\in E
are ordered such that
i<l
if and only if we have
\delta(c_{i}/B)<\delta(c_{l}/B)
. Define
I,O\subseteq E
as follows:

•

If
H
is not
A
-
\vee
-definable, then
I=\emptyset,O=E
.

•

If
G
is not
A
-type-definable, then
I=E,O=\emptyset
.

•

Else,
I=\emptyset
, and
O
is the least final segment of
E
containing the set of indices
o\in E
such that one of the
(c_{ok})_{k}
is Archimedean over
A
.

Either way, let
J=E\setminus(I\cup O)
. Then the following conditions hold:

(1)

For all
i\in I
,
\textup{tp}(c_{i}/B)
has exactly one
\textup{Aut}(M/A)
-invariant global extension
p_{i}
, and it is inner.

(2)

For all
o\in O
,
\textup{tp}(c_{o}/B)
has at least one
\textup{Aut}(M/A)
-invariant global extension, of which exactly one is outer, denote it by
q_{o}
.

(3)

For all
j\in J
,
\textup{tp}(c_{j}/B)
has exactly two
\textup{Aut}(M/A)
-invariant global extensions:
r_{j}
, which is inner, and
s_{j}
, which is outer.

(4)

The map
K\longmapsto(p_{i})_{i\in I},(r_{j})_{j\in K},(s_{j})_{j\in J\setminus K},(q_{o})_{o\in O}
is a bijection from the set of initial segments of
J
to the set of strong
\textup{val}^{3}_{B}
-block extensions of
c
. In particular,
c
admits exactly
|J|+1
strong
\textup{val}^{3}_{B}
-block extensions.

Proof.

Note that
I,O,J
are pairwise-disjoint convex subsets of
E
which cover
E
. In fact,
I
is an initial segment of
E
,
O
is a final segment, and
J
is in-between. The first two conditions are easy consequences of
corollary

3.22
. The third condition also easily follows from the fact that for all
j\in J
, none of the
(c_{jk})_{k}
is Archimedean over
A
. There remains to prove the last condition.

Suppose we have
o\in O
such that
\textup{tp}(c_{o}/B)
has a global
\textup{Aut}(M/A)
-invariant extension
q^{\prime}
which is inner. Let us show that
q^{\prime}
cannot be extended to a strong
\textup{val}^{3}_{B}
-block extension of
c
. Let
(q^{\prime}_{e})_{e\in E}
be some strong block extension of
c
. By definition of
O
, there exists
o^{\prime}
,
k
such that
o^{\prime}\leqslant o
and
c_{o^{\prime}k}
is Archimedean over
A
. As a result,
q_{o^{\prime}}
is the unique global
\textup{Aut}(M/A)
-invariant extension of
\textup{tp}(c_{o^{\prime}}/B)
by
corollary

3.22
, therefore
q^{\prime}_{o^{\prime}}=q_{o^{\prime}}
. As
o^{\prime}\leqslant o
, by
lemma

2.44
,
q^{\prime}_{o}
must be outer, thus
q^{\prime}_{o}\neq q^{\prime}
and we are done.

As a result, all the strong block extensions of
c
extend
(p_{i})_{i\in I},(q_{o})_{o\in O}
. Then, each such extension
(q^{\prime}_{e})_{e}
identifies with a subset of
J
: the set of all
j
such that
q^{\prime}_{j}=r_{j}
. This concludes the proof, as
lemma

2.44
tells us that the valid choices are exactly the initial segments of
J
.
∎

Corollary 3.24
.

If
c
is a finite
\textup{val}^{3}_{B}
-separated family from
M
such that
c\underset{A}{\downfree^{\mathbf{cut}}}B
, then
c
admits a strong
\textup{val}^{3}_{B}
-block extension.

Proof.

By
proposition

2.36
, it is enough to show that each
\textup{val}^{2}_{B}
-block of
c
has a
\textup{val}^{3}_{B}
-block extension. The Archimedean
\textup{val}^{2}_{B}
-blocks of
c
coincide with its Archimedean
\textup{val}^{3}_{B}
-blocks, by definition of
\textup{val}^{3}_{B}
. We find a
\textup{val}^{3}_{B}
-block extension of those blocks by
corollary

3.5
(the valid choices are built explicitly in
proposition

3.4
), and we find a block extension of the
B
-ramified
\textup{val}^{2}_{B}
-blocks by
proposition

3.23
.
∎

We just proved the property
(C4)
, but we are actually very close to get a full classification of the space of global
\textup{Aut}(M/A)
-invariant extensions of
\textup{tp}(c/B)
. We achieve this in the remainder of this section, but these results are not necessary for our main results about forking.

Lemma 3.25
.

Suppose
c=(c_{ij})_{ij}
is a finite
\textup{val}^{2}_{B}
-block of
B
-ramified points from
M
that is
\textup{val}^{3}_{B}
-separated, whose
\textup{val}^{3}_{B}
-blocks are the
(c_{i})_{i}=((c_{ij})_{j})_{i}
. Let
(p_{i})_{i}
be a strong
\textup{val}^{3}_{B}
-block extension of
c
. Then
q=\bigcup\limits_{i}p_{i}\cup\textup{tp}(c/B)
is a complete type in
S(M)
.

Proof.

Suppose the indices
i
are ordered such that
i<k
if and only if we have
\delta(c_{i}/B)<\delta(c_{k}/B)
. Let
b_{ij}\in\textup{ram}(c_{ij}/B)
. We know from
proposition

2.45
that
q
is consistent. Let
\alpha,\beta\models q
. Then each
\alpha_{i},\beta_{i}
is a
\textup{val}^{3}_{M}
-block of
M
-ramified points, and:

\Delta(\alpha_{ij}-b_{ij})=\delta(\alpha_{i}/M),\Delta(\beta_{ij}-b_{ij})=\delta(\beta_{i}/M)

for all
i
and
j
. Moreover, for
i<k
, and arbitrary indices
jl
, we have
\textup{tp}(c/B)\models|x_{kj}-b_{kj}|>n|x_{il}-b_{kl}|
for all
n>0
. It follows that we have
\delta(\alpha_{i}/M)<\delta(\alpha_{k}/M)
and
\delta(\beta_{i}/M)<\delta(\beta_{k}/M)
. We can apply
proposition

2.41
to get
\alpha\equiv_{M}\beta
, which concludes the proof.
∎

Remark 3.26
.

In the setting of
lemma

3.25
, by
proposition

3.23
, the Stone space of all the global
\textup{Aut}(M/A)
-invariant extensions of
\textup{tp}(c/B)
is finite Hausdorff, hence it is discrete.

Remark 3.27
.

Let
X
be a topological space. Suppose
(O_{i})_{i}
is an open partition of
X
. Then the open subsets of
X
are exactly the subsets that can be written
\bigcup\limits_{i}O^{\prime}_{i}
, with
O^{\prime}_{i}
an open subset of
O_{i}
. As a result,
X=\coprod\limits_{i}O_{i}
.

Corollary 3.28
.

Let
X
be a topological space, and
Y
a discrete topological space such that we have a continuous map
X\longrightarrow Y
. Then
X
is the coproduct of the fibers.

Lemma 3.29
.

Suppose
c=(c_{ij})_{ij}(c^{\prime}_{k})_{k}
is a finite,
\textup{val}^{3}_{B}
-separated,
\textup{val}^{1}_{B}
-block such that
c^{\prime}
is the family of its
B
-Archimedean points, and its
B
-ramified
\textup{val}^{3}_{B}
-blocks are the
(c_{i})_{i}=((c_{ij})_{j})_{i}
. Let
(p_{i})_{i}
be a strong
\textup{val}^{3}_{B}
-block extension of
(c_{i})_{i}
, and suppose all the
p_{i}
are outer. Let
G=G(c/B)=G(c^{\prime}/A)
. For each
i
and
j
, choose
b_{ij}\in\textup{ram}(c_{ij}/B)
. Let
d=(b_{ij})_{ij}(c^{\prime}_{k})_{k}
. Let
q_{k}
be a global
\textup{Aut}(M/A)
-invariant extension of
\textup{tp}(c^{\prime}_{k}/B)
, and let
q
be a complete global extension of
\textup{tp}(c/B)
which extends
\bigcup\limits_{i}p_{i}
and
\bigcup\limits_{k}q_{k}
. If
q(x)\models f(x)-f(d)\not\in{\underline{G}_{M}}
for all non-zero
f\in\textup{LC}^{n}(\mathbb{Q})
, then
q
is
\textup{Aut}(M/A)
-invariant.

Proof.

Let
f\in\textup{LC}^{n}(\mathbb{Q})
be non-zero. As is the proof of
lemma

3.3
, it suffices to show that
q(x)\models f(x)\in X
, with
X
some
\textup{Aut}(M/A)
-invariant cut over
M
. By
\textup{val}^{3}_{B}
-separatedness, we have
G(f(c)/B)=G
. Write
f=g+h
, with
g\in\textup{LC}^{|(c_{ij})_{ij}|}(\mathbb{Q})
,
h\in\textup{LC}^{|(c^{\prime}_{k})_{k}|}(\mathbb{Q})
. By
lemma

2.44
,
(p_{i})_{i}
is a strong block extension of
(c_{ij})_{ij}
. By
lemma

3.25
, the pushforward of
q
by the projection over the coordinates
(x_{ij})_{ij}
is
\textup{Aut}(M/A)
-invariant, thus there is
X
an
\textup{Aut}(M/A)
-invariant cut over
M
such that
q(x)\models g((x_{ij})_{ij})\in X
. If
h=0
, then
q(x)\models f(x)\in X
. Now suppose
h\neq 0
. If
g=0
, then we are done as the proof is the same as in
lemma

3.3
, so suppose
g\neq 0
. By
\textup{val}^{3}_{B}
-separatedness,
f(c)
is Archimedean over
B
, and
G(f(c)/B)=G
. By
lemma

2.23
, the two global
\textup{Aut}(M/A)
-invariant extensions of
\textup{tp}(h((c^{\prime}_{k})_{k})/B)
are the translates by
h((c^{\prime}_{k})_{k})
of the connected components of
G\setminus{\underline{G}_{M}}
. By
lemma

3.3
, the pushforward of
q
by the projection on the coordinates
(x^{\prime}_{k})_{k}
is
\textup{Aut}(M/A)
-invariant, therefore there is
Y
one of those two translates so that
q(x)\models h((x^{\prime}_{k})_{k})\in Y
. As the
p_{i}
are outer, and by
\textup{val}^{3}_{B}
-separatedness,
X
is the translate by
g((b_{ij})_{ij})
of some connected component of the type-definable set
G\setminus{\underline{G}_{M}}
. Then, we have
q(x)\models f(x)-f(d)\in G\setminus{\underline{G}_{M}}
, which means that
q(x)\models f(x)\in L\cup R
, with:

L=\textup{ct}_{<}\left(\left[g((b_{ij})_{ij})+h((c^{\prime}_{k})_{k})\textup{ mod }G(M)\right]/M\right)

R=\textup{ct}_{>}\left(\left[g((b_{ij})_{ij})+h((c^{\prime}_{k})_{k})\textup{ mod }G(M)\right]/M\right)

By
A
-invariance of
X
and
Y
, both
L
and
R
are
\textup{Aut}(M/A)
-invariant, and concluding the proof.
∎

Lemma 3.30
.

Suppose
c=(c_{ij})_{ij}(c^{\prime}_{k})_{k}
is a finite,
\textup{val}^{3}_{B}
-separated,
\textup{val}^{1}_{B}
-block such that
c^{\prime}
is the family of its
B
-Archimedean points, and its
B
-ramified
\textup{val}^{3}_{B}
-blocks are the
(c_{i})_{i}=((c_{ij})_{j})_{i}
. Let
(p_{i})_{i}
be a strong
\textup{val}^{3}_{B}
-block extension of
(c_{i})_{i}
. Let
q
be the global
\textup{Aut}(M/A)
-invariant extension of
\textup{tp}((c_{ij})_{ij}/B)
which extends
\bigcup\limits_{i}p_{i}
(
q
exists and is unique, by
lemma

3.25
).

Then the following description yields a full classification of the Stone space
F_{1}
of global
\textup{Aut}(M/A)
-invariant types extending
q\cup\textup{tp}(c/B)
:

Suppose
c^{\prime}
is not empty (or else
q\cup\textup{tp}(c/B)
is already complete). Let
O
be the set of indices
o
such that
p_{o}
is outer. Let
G=G(c/B)=G(c^{\prime}/A)
. Define
{N}
,
H
,
{N}^{\prime}
as in the statement of
proposition

3.4
. For each
i
and
j
, choose
b_{ij}\in\textup{ram}(c_{ij}/B)
, and choose
\alpha
a realization of the projection of
q
onto the coordinates
(x_{o})_{o\in O}
. Let
F_{2}
be the closed subspace of
S_{(x_{o})_{o\in O},x^{\prime}}(\{0\})
corresponding to the types of
\mathbb{Q}-free
families satisfying
\textup{tp}^{{N}^{\prime}}((\alpha_{oj}-b_{oj}\textup{ mod }H)_{oj}/\{0\})
. Then the function mapping
\textup{tp}^{{N}}((\gamma_{ij})_{i\not\in O,j}\alpha(\beta_{k})_{k}/M)
to

\textup{tp}^{{N}^{\prime}}((\alpha_{oj}-b_{oj}\textup{ mod }H)_{o\in O,j}(\beta_{k}-c^{\prime}_{k}\textup{ mod }H)_{k}/\emptyset)

is a well-defined homeomorphism
F_{1}\longrightarrow F_{2}
.

Proof.

Let
d=((b_{oj})_{o\in O,j}(c^{\prime}_{k})_{k})
, and let
\pi
be the projection:

(x_{i})_{i},x^{\prime}\longmapsto(x_{o})_{o\in O},x^{\prime}

Let us show first that the map from the statement factors through a homeomorphism
\pi(F_{1})\longrightarrow F_{2}
. The proof is very similar to that of
proposition

3.4
.

•

Let
\beta\in N
be such that
\textup{tp}(\alpha\beta/M)\in\pi(F_{1})
. Let
f_{1}
in
\textup{LC}^{|\alpha|}(\mathbb{Q})
,
f_{2}
in
\textup{LC}^{|\beta|}(\mathbb{Q})
be such that
f_{1}+f_{2}\neq 0
. Just as in
proposition

3.4
, in order to show that the map
g:\ \pi(F_{1})\longrightarrow F_{2}
given by the statement is well-defined, it suffices to show that we have
f_{1}(\alpha)+f_{2}(\beta)-(f_{1}+f_{2})(d)\not\in{\underline{G}_{M}}
. If
f_{2}\neq 0
, then
(f_{1}+f_{2})(d)
is Archimedean over
A
, which concludes the proof by
lemma

2.23
just as in
proposition

3.4
. As all the
p_{o}
are outer, and
\alpha
is
\textup{val}^{3}_{B}
-separated (as
(c_{oj})_{oj}\equiv_{B}\alpha
is), we have
f_{1}(\alpha)-(f_{1}+0)(d)\not\in{\underline{G}_{M}}
, proving that
g
is well-defined.

•

Let us show that
g
is injective. Let
p_{1}
,
p_{2}\in\pi(F_{1})
be distinct, and
f_{1}
,
f_{2}\in\textup{LC}(\mathbb{Q})
be as in the previous item. We use the same case disjunction (
f_{2}\neq 0
or
f_{2}=0
) as in the previous item to show that the pushforward of
p_{i}
by
f_{1}+f_{2}
is (as a cut over
M
) a translate by
(f_{1}+f_{2})(d)
of one of the two connected components of
G\setminus{\underline{G}_{M}}
. Then, it follows from the same reasoning as in
proposition

3.4
that
g
is injective.

•

Let us now prove surjectivity. Let
\beta^{\prime}
be a tuple from
N^{\prime}
such that
r^{\prime}=\textup{tp}((\alpha_{oj}-b_{oj}\textup{ mod }H)_{oj}\beta^{\prime}/\{0\})\in F_{2}
. Let
\beta
be a preimage of
\beta^{\prime}
by the projection
N\longrightarrow N^{\prime}
. Let
d_{1},d_{2}\in N
be such that we have
\Delta(d_{1})>\Delta(\beta_{i})
,
\Delta(d_{k})>\Delta(\alpha_{oj}-b_{oj})
, and
d_{2}\not\in{\underline{G}_{M}}
for all
i,j,k,o
. Let
\sigma\in\textup{Aut}\left(N/H+\sum\limits_{oj}\mathbb{Q}\cdot(\alpha_{oj}-b_{oj})\right)
be such that
\sigma(|d_{1}|)=|d_{2}|
, and let
r=\textup{tp}(\alpha(c^{\prime}_{k}+\sigma(\beta_{k}))_{k}/M)
. Then
r
is
\textup{Aut}(M/A)
-invariant by
lemma

3.29
, and, just as in
proposition

3.4
,
r
is a preimage of
r^{\prime}
by
g
.

•

We conclude that
g
is a homeomorphism
\pi(F_{1})\longrightarrow F_{2}
.

It remains to show that
F_{1}\longrightarrow\pi(F_{1})
is homeo. By
lemma

3.25
, recall that
p=\textup{tp}((c_{i})_{i\not\in O}/B)\cup\bigcup\limits_{i\not\in O}p_{i}
is a complete global type. Let
q^{\prime}
be in
\pi(F_{1})
. Then, if
\gamma_{1}\models p,\gamma_{2}\models q^{\prime}
, one can note that
G(\gamma_{2}/M)=G
, while
G(\gamma_{1}/M)={\overline{H(c/B)}^{M}}<G
(this is the difference between being inner and outer). As a result, the
\textup{val}^{1}_{M}
(and thus
\textup{val}^{2}_{M}
)-values of the elements of
\gamma_{1}
(there is only one such value, but we do not care) are different from the values of the elements of
\gamma_{2}
. By
proposition

2.36
,
p
and
q
are weakly orthogonal, which shows that
F_{1}\longrightarrow\pi(F_{1})
is homeo.
∎

When put together with
remark

3.26
and
corollary

3.28
, we get that the space of global
\textup{Aut}(M/A)
-invariant extensions of the type of any finite,
\textup{val}^{3}_{B}
-separated
\textup{val}^{1}_{B}
-block
(c_{i})_{i},c^{\prime}
that is cut-independent is the finite coproduct of the topological spaces described in
lemma

3.30
. The continuous map we consider for
corollary

3.28
is the projection
\pi:\ ((x_{i})_{i},x^{\prime})\longmapsto(x_{i})_{i}
. Note that, by
3.30
, any global
\textup{Aut}(M/A)
-invariant extension of the type of
(c_{i})_{i}
over
B
extends to one of the type of
((c_{i})_{i},c^{\prime})
(as one can always extend the type of some
\mathbb{Q}
-free family to an arbitrary type of a larger
\mathbb{Q}
-free family), so
\pi
is onto the finite space described in
remark

3.26
. Now, given a finite,
\textup{val}^{3}_{B}
-separated tuple
c
that is cut-independent, we just described
(S_{i})_{i}
, the Stone spaces of the global
\textup{Aut}(M/A)
-invariant extensions of the type of each
\textup{val}^{1}_{B}
-block of
c
. By
(C7)
and
lemma

1.33
, the Stone space of every global
\textup{Aut}(M/A)
-invariant extension of the type of the full tuple
c
is exactly the finite direct product of the
S_{i}
. Let us rephrase all of these conclusions (with their hypothesis) in a single statement for good measure:

Remark 3.31
.

In DOAG, let
M
be a sufficiently saturated and strongly homogeneous model, and
C\geqslant A\leqslant B
be
\mathbb{Q}
-vector subspaces, such that

C\underset{A}{\downfree^{\mathbf{cut}}}B
. Let
c=(c_{ij})_{ij}
be some
\textup{val}^{3}_{B}
-separated family of
C
, with
(c_{i})_{i}=((c_{ij})_{j})_{i}
its
\textup{val}^{1}_{B}
-blocks. For each
i
, let
d_{i}
be the tuple of
B
-ramified points of
c_{i}
. Let
F_{i}
be the space of all global
\textup{Aut}(M/A)
-invariant extensions of
\textup{tp}(d_{i}/B)
, which is finite, and fully described by
proposition

3.23
and
lemma

3.25
. For each
i
, and for each
q\in F_{i}
, let
S_{q}
be the space (described in
lemma

3.30
) of global
\textup{Aut}(M/A)
-invariant extensions of
\textup{tp}(c_{i}/B)
which extend
q
. We recall that
S_{i}
is homeomorphic to some closed set of parameter-free DOAG-types. Then the space
S_{i}
of all global
\textup{Aut}(M/A)
-invariant extensions of
\textup{tp}(c_{i}/B)
is of course the finite disjoint union
\bigcup\limits_{q\in F_{i}}S_{q}
, which is naturally homeomorphic to the coproduct
\coprod\limits_{q\in F_{i}}S_{q}
. Lastly, the whole space of global
\textup{Aut}(M/A)
-invariant extensions of
\textup{tp}(c/B)
is naturally homeomorphic to
\prod\limits_{i}S_{i}
.

In conclusion, we established a natural and explicit homeomorphism between the space of global
\textup{Aut}(M/A)
-invariant extensions of
\textup{tp}(c/B)
, and a topological space of the form
\prod\limits_{i}\coprod\limits_{j}F_{ij}
, with
(F_{ij})_{ij}
some closed sets of parameter-free DOAG-types.

Moreover, as definable bijections are homeomorphisms in
S(M)
, this homeomorphism type is kept when replacing
c
by an
A
-interdefinable tuple. Note that we show in the next section that every cut-independent tuple is
A
-interdefinable with a
\textup{val}^{3}_{B}
-separated, cut-independent family. As a result, this will yield a complete description of the Stone space of global
\textup{Aut}(M/A)
-invariant extensions of any non-forking type.

4.
Normal forms

We built in the last two sections a nice framework to get a fine understanding of a particular class of tuples, the
\textup{val}^{3}_{B}
-separated families. What remains for us to do in order to prove
theorem

1.14
is to prove
(C3)
, which allows us to always reduce to the case where we deal with such a nice family.

We adopt
1.37
in this section.

We have to show that
c
is
A
-interdefinable with a
\textup{val}^{3}_{B}
-separated family
d
, which is cut-independent by
remark

1.36
. In fact,
d
will also be
\textup{val}^{3}_{A}
-separated. This could be useful, as we saw in
proposition

3.23
that we have to keep track of which points from
d
are Archimedean or ramified over
A
in order to have a good understanding of the global
\textup{Aut}(M/A)
-invariant extensions of its type. By
remark

1.38
,
d
is the image of
c
by a composition of
A
-translations, and a map from GL
{}_{n}(\mathbb{Q})
. In order to build
d
, we start with a particular basis of
\textup{dcl}(Ac)
(remember that the definable closure of a set is the
\mathbb{Q}
-vector subspace generated by said set in DOAG), then we go through a process of several steps, where we apply
A
-translations and maps from GL
{}_{n}(\mathbb{Q})
to this basis. At the end of each step, our current tuple satisfies an additional property from a list of conditions, the conjunction of which is a sufficient condition to be a
\textup{val}^{3}_{B}
and
\textup{val}^{3}_{A}
-separated family.

Remark 4.1
.

Recall that, if
d\in M
is a
B
-Archimedean singleton such that
d\underset{A}{\downfree^{\mathbf{cut}}}B
, then
d
must be Archimedean over
A
(see
remark

3.1
). In particular,
G(d/B)=G(d/A)
.

The following definition allows us to enumerate our families in a way that helps us to simultaneously consider their
\textup{val}^{3}_{B}
-blocks and their
\textup{val}^{3}_{A}
-blocks. The elements of our families are indexed by the subgroups
G(-/B)
, and the eventual Archimedean classes
\delta(-/B)
that they bring. We use the single quote
′
to refer to elements that are Archimedean over
A
and ramified over
B
. We use the tilde
\tilde{\ }
to refer to elements that are ramified over
A
(and thus over
B
if the family is cut-independent from
B
over
A
). By elimination, the other elements are those that are Archimedean over
B
and
A
.

Definition 4.2
.

We say that the family
(d_{Gi})_{Gi}(d^{\prime}_{G^{\prime}\delta^{\prime}i^{\prime}})_{G^{\prime}\delta^{\prime}i^{\prime}}(\tilde{d}_{\tilde{G}\tilde{\delta}\tilde{i}})_{\tilde{G}\tilde{\delta}\tilde{i}}
is
under normal enumeration
(with respect to
A
and
B
) if the following conditions hold:

•

All the
d_{Gi}
are Archimedean over
B
.

•

All the
d^{\prime}_{G^{\prime}\delta^{\prime}i^{\prime}}
are Archimedean over
A
and ramified over
B
.

•

All the
\tilde{d}_{\tilde{G}\tilde{\delta}\tilde{i}}
are ramified over
A
.

•

For all
G
,
i
, we have
G(d_{Gi}/B)=G
.

•

For all
G^{\prime}
,
\delta^{\prime}
,
i^{\prime}
, we have
G(d^{\prime}_{G^{\prime}\delta^{\prime}i^{\prime}}/B)=G^{\prime}
and
\delta(d^{\prime}_{G^{\prime}\delta^{\prime}i^{\prime}}/B)=\delta^{\prime}
.

•

For all
\tilde{G}
,
\tilde{\delta}
,
\tilde{i}
, we have
G(\tilde{d}_{\tilde{G}\tilde{\delta}\tilde{i}}/B)=\tilde{G}
, and
\delta(\tilde{d}_{\tilde{G}\tilde{\delta}\tilde{i}}/A)=\tilde{\delta}
.

Given a normal enumeration, the ramified
\textup{val}^{3}_{B}
-blocks of the family are
((d^{\prime}_{G^{\prime}\delta^{\prime}i^{\prime}})_{i^{\prime}}(\tilde{d}_{G^{\prime}\delta^{\prime}\tilde{i}})_{\tilde{i}})_{G^{\prime}\delta^{\prime}}
, its Archimedean
\textup{val}^{3}_{B}
-blocks are
((d_{Gi})_{i})_{G}
, its ramified
\textup{val}^{3}_{A}
-blocks are
((\tilde{d}_{\tilde{G}\tilde{\delta}\tilde{i}})_{\tilde{i}})_{\tilde{G}\tilde{\delta}}
, and its Archimedean
\textup{val}^{3}_{A}
-blocks are
((d_{Gi})_{i}(d^{\prime}_{G\delta^{\prime}i^{\prime}})_{\delta^{\prime}i^{\prime}})_{G}
. One can note that the presence of the elements of
d^{\prime}
is the reason why none of the valuations
\textup{val}^{3}_{A}
,
\textup{val}^{3}_{B}
refines the other.

Let us now give the list of properties that we want to satisfy:

Definition 4.3
.

Let
dd^{\prime}\tilde{d}
be a family under normal enumeration with respect to
A
and
B
. We say that
dd^{\prime}\tilde{d}
is
under normal form
with respect to
A
and
B
if it is a lift of a
\mathbb{Q}
-free family from
{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.41666pt}{$\displaystyle{M}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.41666pt}{$\displaystyle{B}$}}{\raisebox{3.41666pt}{$\textstyle{M}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.41666pt}{$\textstyle{B}$}}{\raisebox{2.39166pt}{$\scriptstyle{M}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.39166pt}{$\scriptstyle{B}$}}{\raisebox{1.70833pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{M}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.70833pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{B}$}}}
, and the following conditions hold:

(P1)

For all
\tilde{G}
and
\tilde{\delta}
,
(\tilde{d}_{\tilde{G}\tilde{\delta}\tilde{i}})_{\tilde{i}}
is a lift of a
\mathbb{Q}
-free family from
{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.41666pt}{$\displaystyle{M_{\leqslant\tilde{\delta}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.41666pt}{$\displaystyle{M_{<\tilde{\delta}}}$}}{\raisebox{3.41666pt}{$\textstyle{M_{\leqslant\tilde{\delta}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.41666pt}{$\textstyle{M_{<\tilde{\delta}}}$}}{\raisebox{2.39166pt}{$\scriptstyle{M_{\leqslant\tilde{\delta}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.39166pt}{$\scriptstyle{M_{<\tilde{\delta}}}$}}{\raisebox{1.70833pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{M_{\leqslant\tilde{\delta}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.70833pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{M_{<\tilde{\delta}}}$}}}
.

(P2)

Non-trivial linear combinations of
dd^{\prime}
are Archimedean over
A
.

(P3)

For all
G
, non-trivial linear combinations
e
of
(d_{Gi})_{i}(d^{\prime}_{G\delta^{\prime}i^{\prime}})_{\delta^{\prime}i^{\prime}}
satisfy
G(e/A)=G
.

(P4)

For all
G
, any non-trivial linear combination
c
of the tuple
(d_{Gi})_{i}
is Archimedean over
B
(which implies
G(c/B)=G
if the family is cut-independent, by
(P3)
and
remark

4.1
).

(P5)

For all
G^{\prime}
and
\delta^{\prime}
, non-trivial linear combinations
e^{\prime}
of the tuple
(d^{\prime}_{G^{\prime}\delta^{\prime}i^{\prime}})_{i^{\prime}}(\tilde{d}_{G^{\prime}\delta^{\prime}\tilde{i}})_{\tilde{i}}
are ramified over
B
, and satisfy
\delta(e^{\prime}/B)=\delta^{\prime}
.

Remark 4.4
.

Condition
(P1)
implies that each ramified
\textup{val}^{3}_{A}
-block is
\textup{val}^{3}_{A}
-separated. The conjunction of
(P2)
and
(P3)
is equivalent to each Archimedean
\textup{val}^{3}_{A}
-block being
\textup{val}^{3}_{A}
-separated. Likewise,
(P4)
(resp.
(P5)
) is equivalent to each Archimedean (resp. ramified)
\textup{val}^{3}_{B}
-block being
\textup{val}^{3}_{B}
-separated. As a result, if we manage to prove that
c
is
A
-interdefinable with a family under normal form, then this family would be simultaneously
\textup{val}^{3}_{A}
-separated and
\textup{val}^{3}_{B}
-separated, and
(C3)
would follow.

Remark 4.5
.

From the definition of the normal form, if
dd^{\prime}\tilde{d}
is under normal form with respect to
A
and
B
, then one can note that any subfamily of
dd^{\prime}\tilde{d}
is also under normal form with respect to
A
and
B
.

Definition 4.6
.

Define
C
to be the
\mathbb{Q}
-vector space generated by
Ac
. We define a
basis
(resp.
free family
)
of
C
over
A
as a lift of a basis (resp. free family) of
{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.41666pt}{$\displaystyle{C}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.41666pt}{$\displaystyle{A}$}}{\raisebox{3.41666pt}{$\textstyle{C}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.41666pt}{$\textstyle{A}$}}{\raisebox{2.39166pt}{$\scriptstyle{C}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.39166pt}{$\scriptstyle{A}$}}{\raisebox{1.70833pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{C}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.70833pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{A}$}}}
.

Remark 4.7
.

Remember that
dim\left({\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.41666pt}{$\displaystyle{C}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.41666pt}{$\displaystyle{A}$}}{\raisebox{3.41666pt}{$\textstyle{C}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.41666pt}{$\textstyle{A}$}}{\raisebox{2.39166pt}{$\scriptstyle{C}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.39166pt}{$\scriptstyle{A}$}}{\raisebox{1.70833pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{C}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.70833pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{A}$}}}\right)
is finite. It is easy to see that any concatenation of lifts of bases of
{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.41666pt}{$\displaystyle{C_{\leqslant\tilde{\delta}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.41666pt}{$\displaystyle{C_{<\tilde{\delta}}}$}}{\raisebox{3.41666pt}{$\textstyle{C_{\leqslant\tilde{\delta}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.41666pt}{$\textstyle{C_{<\tilde{\delta}}}$}}{\raisebox{2.39166pt}{$\scriptstyle{C_{\leqslant\tilde{\delta}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.39166pt}{$\scriptstyle{C_{<\tilde{\delta}}}$}}{\raisebox{1.70833pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{C_{\leqslant\tilde{\delta}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.70833pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{C_{<\tilde{\delta}}}$}}}
, with
\tilde{\delta}\in\Delta(C)\setminus\Delta(A)
, is a free family over
A
, hence a finite family. In particular,
\Delta(C)\setminus\Delta(A)
is finite.

Lemma 4.8
.

For each
\tilde{\delta}\in\Delta(C)\setminus\Delta(A)
, let
\tilde{d}_{\tilde{\delta}}
be a lift of a basis of
{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.41666pt}{$\displaystyle{C_{\leqslant\tilde{\delta}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.41666pt}{$\displaystyle{C_{<\tilde{\delta}}}$}}{\raisebox{3.41666pt}{$\textstyle{C_{\leqslant\tilde{\delta}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.41666pt}{$\textstyle{C_{<\tilde{\delta}}}$}}{\raisebox{2.39166pt}{$\scriptstyle{C_{\leqslant\tilde{\delta}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.39166pt}{$\scriptstyle{C_{<\tilde{\delta}}}$}}{\raisebox{1.70833pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{C_{\leqslant\tilde{\delta}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.70833pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{C_{<\tilde{\delta}}}$}}}
, and let
\tilde{d}
be the concatenation of all the
\tilde{d}_{\tilde{\delta}}
. Then, for all
e
in
C\setminus\textup{dcl}(A\tilde{d})
, there exists
\tilde{e}\in\textup{dcl}(A\tilde{d})
such that
e+\tilde{e}
is Archimedean over
A
.

Moreover, if
e
is ramified over
A
, then we can choose
\tilde{e}
such that there exists
\tilde{\delta}\in\Delta(C)\setminus\Delta(A)
for which
\Delta(e+\tilde{e})<\tilde{\delta}\leqslant\Delta(e)
.

Proof.

Let us build
\tilde{e}
by induction. Let
\tilde{e}_{0}=0\in\textup{dcl}(A\tilde{d})
. If
e+\tilde{e}_{n}
is not Archimedean over
A
, then let
a\in\textup{ram}(e+\tilde{e}_{n}/A)
, and
\tilde{\delta}_{n}=\delta(e+\tilde{e}_{n}/A)
. By definition of
\tilde{d}
, there exists
v
a linear combination of
\tilde{d}_{\tilde{\delta}_{n}}
for which we have
\Delta(e+\tilde{e}_{n}-a-v)<\tilde{\delta}_{n}
, so let us set
\tilde{e}_{n+1}=\tilde{e}_{n}-a-v
.

By finiteness of
\Delta(C)\setminus\Delta(A)
, the sequence of the
\tilde{\delta}_{n}
, and hence that of the
\tilde{e}_{n}
, must eventually stop. The ultimate value
\tilde{e}
of the
\tilde{e}_{n}
witnesses the first part of the statement. If
e
is ramified over
A
, then
\tilde{e}\neq 0=\tilde{e}_{0}
, so
\tilde{\delta}_{0}
exists and witnesses the second part of the statement.
∎

Lemma 4.9
.

For each
\tilde{\delta}\in\Delta(C)\setminus\Delta(A)
, let
\tilde{d}_{\tilde{\delta}}
be a lift of a basis of
{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.41666pt}{$\displaystyle{C_{\leqslant\tilde{\delta}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.41666pt}{$\displaystyle{C_{<\tilde{\delta}}}$}}{\raisebox{3.41666pt}{$\textstyle{C_{\leqslant\tilde{\delta}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.41666pt}{$\textstyle{C_{<\tilde{\delta}}}$}}{\raisebox{2.39166pt}{$\scriptstyle{C_{\leqslant\tilde{\delta}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.39166pt}{$\scriptstyle{C_{<\tilde{\delta}}}$}}{\raisebox{1.70833pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{C_{\leqslant\tilde{\delta}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.70833pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{C_{<\tilde{\delta}}}$}}}
, and let
\tilde{d}
be the concatenation of all the
\tilde{d}_{\tilde{\delta}}
. Let
(v_{n})_{n}
be a sequence such that
(\tilde{d},v_{n})
is a basis of
C
over
A
for each
n
. Suppose that, for all
n
, there exists
v_{1}^{\prime}
a term from
v_{n}
,
v_{2}^{\prime}
a term from
v_{n+1}
, and
\delta\in\Delta(C)\setminus\Delta(A)
, such that
\Delta(v_{2}^{\prime})<\delta\leqslant\Delta(v_{1}^{\prime})
, and
v_{n+1}
is obtained from
v_{n}
by replacing
v_{1}^{\prime}
by
v_{2}^{\prime}
.

Then the sequence
(v_{n})_{n}
must eventually stop.

Proof.

Let
N=|\Delta(C)\setminus\Delta(A)|<\omega
,
(\delta_{n})_{n<N}
the strictly increasing enumeration of
\Delta(C)\setminus\Delta(A)
, and
\mathcal{I}
be the set of intervals:

\left\{\left]-\infty,\delta_{0}\right[,\left[\delta_{0},\delta_{1}\right[,\left[\delta_{1},\delta_{2}\right[,\ldots\left[\delta_{N-2},\delta_{N-1}\right[,\left[\delta_{N-1},+\infty\right[\right\}

Define on
\mathcal{I}
the only total order that makes the canonical projection
f:\ \Delta(C)\longrightarrow\mathcal{I}
an order-preserving map. With respect to that order, let
g
be the unique order-isomorphism
\mathcal{I}\longrightarrow\{0,1\ldots N\}
. For each
i
between
0
and
N
, let
F(n,i)
be the number of terms
v
from
v_{n}
such that
g(f(\Delta(v)))=i
. Finally, let
\rho
be the following function:

n\longmapsto F(n,N)\times\omega^{N}+F(n,N-1)\times\omega^{N-1}+\ldots+F(n,1)\times\omega+F(n,0)

then
\rho
has its values in the well-ordered set
\omega^{N+1}
. By hypothesis, for each
n
, there exists
m<m^{\prime}
such that
F(n,m^{\prime})=F(n+1,m^{\prime})+1
,
F(n,m)+1=F(n+1,m)
, and
F(n,i)=F(n+1,i)
for every
i\neq m
,
m^{\prime}
. As a result,
\rho
is strictly decreasing, so the well-ordering forces the sequence
(v_{n})_{n}
to stop eventually.
∎

Lemma 4.10
.

For each
\tilde{\delta}\in\Delta(C)\setminus\Delta(A)
, let
u_{\tilde{\delta}}
be a lift of a basis of
{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.41666pt}{$\displaystyle{C_{\leqslant\tilde{\delta}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.41666pt}{$\displaystyle{C_{<\tilde{\delta}}}$}}{\raisebox{3.41666pt}{$\textstyle{C_{\leqslant\tilde{\delta}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.41666pt}{$\textstyle{C_{<\tilde{\delta}}}$}}{\raisebox{2.39166pt}{$\scriptstyle{C_{\leqslant\tilde{\delta}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.39166pt}{$\scriptstyle{C_{<\tilde{\delta}}}$}}{\raisebox{1.70833pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{C_{\leqslant\tilde{\delta}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.70833pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{C_{<\tilde{\delta}}}$}}}
, and let
u
be the concatenation of all the
u_{\tilde{\delta}}
. Then there exists
dd^{\prime}\tilde{d}
a basis of
C
over
A
, under normal enumeration with respect to
A
and
B
, for which
(P2)
holds, and
\tilde{d}=u
(so
(P1)
holds as well).

Proof.

Let
w_{0}
be some basis of
C
over
A
containing
u
. Note that
w_{n}=(u,v_{n})
does not witness the statement if and only if there exists a non-trivial linear combination
v^{\prime}
of
v_{n}
that is ramified over
A
. In that case, let
v
be some term of
v_{n}
that appears in
v^{\prime}
, chosen such that
\Delta(v)
is maximal. By valuation inequality, we must have
\Delta(v)\geqslant\Delta(v^{\prime})
. Apply
lemma

4.8
to find
u^{\prime}\in\textup{dcl}(Au)
and
\delta\in\Delta(C)\setminus\Delta(A)
for which
\Delta(v^{\prime}+u^{\prime})<\delta\leqslant\Delta(v^{\prime})\leqslant\Delta(v)
. Replace
v
by
v^{\prime}+u^{\prime}
in
w_{n}
, let
w_{n+1}
be the new family obtained. Now,
w_{n+1}
is also a basis of
C
over
A
containing
u
.

The sequence
(v_{n})_{n}
witnesses the hypothesis of
lemma

4.9
, so it must eventually stop, and its last element witnesses the statement.
∎

Remark 4.11
.

If
w=(u,v)
witnesses
lemma

4.10
, and
M
is an invertible matrix of size
|v|
with coefficients in
\mathbb{Q}
, then
(u,(Mv))
also witnesses
lemma

4.10
.

Lemma 4.12
.

Let
dd^{\prime}\tilde{d}=(v,\tilde{d})
be a free family over
A
that witnesses
(P1)
and
(P2)
, and
N=|v|
. Then there exists
M\in\textup{GL}_{N}(\mathbb{Q})
such that
((Mv)
,
\tilde{d})
witnesses
(P3)
(and thus
(P1)
,
(P2)
by
remark

4.11
).

Proof.

This is by induction over
N
.
(P3)
trivially holds if
N=0,1
.

For every
G
, let
v_{G}
be the
\textup{val}^{1}_{A}
-block of
v
of value
G
. Let
G
be maximal such that
v_{G}
is not
\textup{val}^{3}_{A}
-separated. As long as
v_{G}
is not
\textup{val}^{3}_{A}
-separated, there must exist
e
a non-trivial linear combination of
v_{G}
such that
G(e/A)<G
, then replace some term of
v_{G}
that appears in
e
by
e
(making that replacement corresponds to replacing
v
by
Mv
for some
M\in\textup{GL}_{N}(\mathbb{Q})
). As we have
G(e/A)<G
, these replacements always decrease the size of
v_{G}
, and leave
v_{>G}
untouched as the value of
e
is smaller, so the
\textup{val}^{1}_{A}
-blocks of value larger than
G
remain separated, thus we keep the maximality of
G
. As a result, these replacements eventually stop, and they stop before we removed all the terms from
v_{G}
, because if there is only one term left in
v_{G}
, then
v_{G}
is clearly separated.

By induction hypothesis, we can replace
v_{<G}
by
Mv_{<G}
such that the family
((Mv_{<G}),\tilde{d})
witnesses
(P3)
. It is now clear that
((Mv_{<G}),v_{\geqslant G},\tilde{d})
witnesses
(P3)
. The only changes we made to the original family
v
are operations from
\textup{GL}_{N}(\mathbb{Q})
, concluding the proof.
∎

Remark 4.13
.

Let
w=dd^{\prime}\tilde{d}=(v,\tilde{d})
be a free family over
A
that witnesses
(P1)
,
(P2)
,
(P3)
. Let
v_{G}
be the
\textup{val}^{1}_{A}
-block of
v
of value
G
. If we replace
v_{G}
by
Mv_{G}
in
w
for some invertible matrix
M
, then the new family
w^{\prime}
still witnesses
(P1)
,
(P2)
and
(P3)
.

Lemma 4.14
.

There exists a basis of
C
over
A
that witnesses
(P1)
,
(P2)
,
(P3)
,
(P4)
.

Proof.

Let
w=dd^{\prime}\tilde{d}
be a basis of
C
over
A
that witnesses
(P1)
,
(P2)
and
(P3)
. For each
G
, as long as there is a non-trivial linear combination
e
of the
(d_{Gi})_{i}
that is not Archimedean over
B
, then replace some term of
(d_{Gi})_{i}
that appears in
e
by
e
. Each of these replacements removes a term from
(d_{Gi})_{i}
and adds a new term to
(d^{\prime}_{G\delta^{\prime}i^{\prime}})_{\delta^{\prime}i^{\prime}}
. This eventually stops as the number of terms of
(d_{Gi})_{i}
is finite and strictly decreases at each step.

By
remark

4.13
, the new family
w^{\prime}
that we obtain after all these replacements still witnesses
(P1)
,
(P2)
,
(P3)
. We perform these replacements for each
G
, and the new family obtained clearly witnesses
(P4)
.
∎

Remark 4.15
.

Let
w=dd^{\prime}\tilde{d}
be a free family over
A
that witnesses
(P1)
,
(P2)
,
(P3)
,
(P4)
, and
G^{\prime}
some index. Then, if we replace
(d^{\prime}_{G^{\prime}\delta^{\prime}i^{\prime}})_{\delta^{\prime}i^{\prime}}
by
M(d^{\prime}_{G^{\prime}\delta^{\prime}i^{\prime}})_{\delta^{\prime}i^{\prime}}
in
w
for some invertible matrix
M
, it is clear that the new family still witnesses
(P1)
,
(P2)
,
(P3)
,
(P4)
.

Lemma 4.16
.

Let
w=dd^{\prime}\tilde{d}
be a basis of
C
over
A
that witnesses
(P1)
,
(P2)
,
(P3)
,
(P4)
. Let
d^{\prime}_{G^{\prime}\delta^{\prime}k^{\prime}}
be a term from
d^{\prime}
, and
\tilde{e}
be a linear combination of the
(\tilde{d}_{G^{\prime}\tilde{\delta}\tilde{i}})_{\tilde{\delta}\tilde{i}}
. If we replace
d^{\prime}_{G^{\prime}\delta^{\prime}k^{\prime}}
by
e=d^{\prime}_{G^{\prime}\delta^{\prime}k^{\prime}}+\tilde{e}
in
w
, then the new family obtained is still a basis of
C
over
A
that witnesses
(P1)
,
(P2)
,
(P3)
,
(P4)
.

Proof.

First of all, as
\textup{val}^{2}_{A}(d^{\prime}_{G^{\prime}\delta^{\prime}k^{\prime}})>\textup{val}^{2}_{A}(\tilde{e})
,
e
is Archimedean over
A
, and
G(e/A)=G^{\prime}
. As
\textup{val}^{2}_{B}(e)\leqslant\max(\textup{val}^{2}_{B}(d^{\prime}_{G^{\prime}\delta^{\prime}k^{\prime}}),\textup{val}^{2}_{B}(\tilde{e}))
,
e
cannot be Archimedean over
B
. This implies that the
A
-ramified and
B
-Archimedean blocks of our new family are left untouched, hence we keep the properties
(P1)
,
(P4)
. Let
u^{\prime}
be the new block of
A
-Archimedean and
B
-ramified points in the normal enumeration. Then
e
lies in the block
u^{\prime}_{G^{\prime}}
. In order to show that
(P2)
and
(P3)
hold in the new family
du^{\prime}\tilde{d}
, we just have to show that the
\textup{val}^{3}_{A}
-block
d_{G^{\prime}}u_{G^{\prime}}^{\prime}
is
\textup{val}^{3}_{A}
-separated. Indeed, it would follow that the whole
du^{\prime}
is
\textup{val}^{3}_{A}
-separated, which is clearly equivalent to
(P2)
and
(P3)
being true in
du^{\prime}\tilde{d}
.

Any non-trivial linear combination of
d_{G^{\prime}}u^{\prime}_{G^{\prime}}
can be written
u+\lambda\cdot\tilde{e}
, with
u
a non-trivial linear combination of
d_{G^{\prime}}d^{\prime}_{G^{\prime}}
, and
\lambda\in\mathbb{Q}
. As
(P2)
and
(P3)
hold in
dd^{\prime}\tilde{d}
, we have
G(u/A)=G^{\prime}
, and
u
is Archimedean over
A
. Then, we have
\textup{val}^{3}_{A}(u)>\textup{val}^{3}_{A}(\tilde{e})
, hence
\textup{val}^{3}_{A}(u+\lambda\cdot\tilde{e})=\textup{val}^{3}_{A}(u)
. This value equals that of the corresponding block,
\textup{val}^{3}_{A}(d_{G^{\prime}}d^{\prime}_{G^{\prime}})
, as
(P2)
and
(P3)
hold in
dd^{\prime}\tilde{d}
, thus it equals
\textup{val}^{3}_{A}(d_{G^{\prime}}u^{\prime}_{G^{\prime}})
, concluding the proof.
∎

Lemma 4.17
.

Let
w=dd^{\prime}\tilde{d}
be a basis of
C
over
A
that witnesses
(P1)
,
(P2)
,
(P3)
,
(P4)
, and not
(P5)
. Then there exists
G^{\prime}
,
\delta^{\prime}
, and two
\mathbb{Q}
-linear maps
f,g\in\textup{LC}(\mathbb{Q})
, such that
v=f((d^{\prime}_{G^{\prime}\delta^{\prime}i^{\prime}})_{i^{\prime}})+g((\tilde{d}_{G^{\prime}\delta^{\prime}\tilde{i}})_{\tilde{i}})
is ramified over
B
, and
\delta(v/B)<\delta^{\prime}
.

Moreover, if
d^{\prime}_{G^{\prime}\delta^{\prime}k^{\prime}}
appears in
v
, then replacing it by
v
in
w
gives a new family
w^{\prime}
which is still a basis of
C
over
A
that witnesses
(P1)
,
(P2)
,
(P3)
,
(P4)
.

Proof.

By definition of
(P5)
, there exists
G^{\prime}
,
\delta^{\prime}
, and
\mathbb{Q}
-linear maps
f,g
in
\textup{LC}(\mathbb{Q})
such that, either
v=f((d^{\prime}_{G^{\prime}\delta^{\prime}i^{\prime}})_{i^{\prime}})+g((\tilde{d}_{G^{\prime}\delta^{\prime}\tilde{i}})_{\tilde{i}})
is not ramified over
B
, or
\delta(v/B)\neq\delta^{\prime}
.

Note that
f
must be non-zero, otherwise the above hypothesis fails by
(P1)
. By
(P1)
,
(P2)
,
(P3)
,
dd^{\prime}\tilde{d}
is
\textup{val}^{3}_{A}
-separated. As
f
is non-zero,
\textup{val}^{3}_{A}(v)=\textup{val}^{3}_{A}(d^{\prime}_{G^{\prime}})
, thus
v
is Archimedean over
A
, and
G(v/A)=G^{\prime}=G(v/B)
. For all
i^{\prime}
,
\tilde{i}
, we have
d^{\prime}_{G^{\prime}\delta^{\prime}i^{\prime}},\tilde{d}_{G^{\prime}\delta^{\prime}\tilde{i}}\in B+G^{\prime}
(by the definition of being ramified over
B
), so
v\in B+G^{\prime}=B+G(v/B)
, therefore
v
must be ramified over
B
, thus by hypothesis we have
\delta(v/B)\neq\delta^{\prime}
. By valuation inequality (for
\textup{val}^{3}_{B}
), we must have
\delta(v/B)<\delta^{\prime}
, and we get the first part of the statement.

Suppose that
d^{\prime}_{G^{\prime}\delta^{\prime}k^{\prime}}
appears in
v
. Then, replacing
d^{\prime}_{G^{\prime}\delta^{\prime}k^{\prime}}
by
v^{\prime}=f((d^{\prime}_{G^{\prime}\delta^{\prime}i^{\prime}})_{i^{\prime}})
is just a replacement of
(d^{\prime}_{G^{\prime}\gamma^{\prime}i^{\prime}})_{\gamma^{\prime}i^{\prime}}
by
M(d^{\prime}_{G^{\prime}\gamma^{\prime}i^{\prime}})_{\gamma^{\prime}i^{\prime}}
for some invertible matrix
M
. By
remark

4.15
, the new family is still a basis of
C
over
A
that witnesses
(P1)
,
(P2)
,
(P3)
,
(P4)
. By
(P2)
and
(P3)
,
v^{\prime}
is Archimedean over
A
, and
G(v^{\prime}/A)=G^{\prime}
. Moreover, as all the
(d^{\prime}_{G^{\prime}\delta^{\prime}i^{\prime}})_{i^{\prime}}
are in
B+G^{\prime}
,
v^{\prime}
must be ramified over
B
. Then,
w^{\prime}
is obtained by replacing
v^{\prime}
by
v
in (a normal enumeration of)
w^{\prime\prime}
. This is exactly the operation described in
lemma

4.16
, concluding the proof.
∎

Theorem 4.18
.

There exists
w=dd^{\prime}\tilde{d}
a basis of
C
over
A
that is under normal form with respect to
A
and
B
.

Proof.

Let
w_{0}=dd^{\prime 0}\tilde{d}
be a basis of
C
over
A
that witnesses
(P1)
,
(P2)
,
(P3)
,
(P4)
.

Suppose
w_{n}=dd^{\prime n}\tilde{d}
does not witness
(P5)
. Then the hypothesis of
lemma

4.17
hold in
w_{n}
, so we do the replacement from this lemma. Let
w_{n+1}
be the new family obtained.

To prove the theorem, we have to prove that the sequence
(w_{n})_{n}
must stop.

To do this, we once again build a strictly decreasing map with values in
\omega^{\omega}
. Let
N=|\Delta(C+B)\setminus\Delta(B)|
, and
(\delta_{i})_{i<N}
be the strictly increasing enumeration of
\Delta(CB)\setminus\Delta(B)
. Define
F(n,i)
as the number of terms
c
from
d^{\prime n}
for which
\delta(c/B)=\delta_{i}
. We define
g
as the map:

n\longmapsto F(n,N)\times\omega^{N}+F(n,N-1)\times\omega^{N-1}+\ldots+F(n,1)\times\omega+F(n,0)

For each
n
, the family
w_{n+1}
is obtained from
w_{n}
by replacing some term
v
from
d^{\prime n}
by a vector
v^{\prime}
from
d^{\prime n+1}
for which
\delta(v^{\prime}/B)<\delta(v/B)
, so
g
is strictly decreasing. This concludes the proof.
∎

With that, we proved
theorem

1.14
, the main technical theorem of this paper stating that
\underset{}{\downfree^{\mathbf{cut}}}=\underset{}{\downfree^{\mathbf{inv}}}
in DOAG. The reasoning is explained in the end of
section

1
, and the different steps of the proof are carried out in
proposition

2.32
,
proposition

2.36
,
proposition

2.45
,
corollary

3.24
and
theorem

4.18
.

5.
Quantifier-free types in the Presburger language

Definition 5.1
.

The
Presburger language
is defined as :

\mathcal{L}_{P}=\left\{+,-,<,0,\text{1},(\mathfrak{d}_{l^{n}})_{l\textup{ prime},n>0}\right\}

Given an ordered Abelian group
G
, we see
G
as an
\mathcal{L}_{P}
-structure by setting
\mathfrak{d}_{l^{n}}(x)
as the predicate
\exists y,\ l^{n}y=x
, and setting
\text{1}=\min(]0,+\infty[)
if
G
is discrete, else we interpret
1
as the only
\emptyset
-definable choice,
\text{1}=0
(this is the standard interpretation of the language). A
special
subgroup of
G
is a pure subgroup (that is a relatively divisible subgroup)
A\leqslant G
containing
1
. Special subgroups will correspond to definably closed sets in the groups we are interested in (see
corollary

6.6
).

Define
S^{m}_{\textup{qf}}(A)
to be the Stone space of quantifier-free types over
A
in
m
variables. Given an
m
-tuple
c
, we write
\textup{tp}_{l}(c/A)
as the partial type generated by the set of quantifier-free formulas satisfied by
c
, with parameters in
A
, which only involve the predicates
(\mathfrak{d}_{l^{n}})_{n>0}
(equality does not appear in these formulas either). We write
S^{m}_{l}(A)
for the Stone space of all such partial types, and we call its elements the
l
-types
. We define similarly
\textup{tp}_{<}(c/A)
,
S^{m}_{<}(A)
for the quantifier-free formulas which only involve the predicates
=
,
<
.

For ease of notation, define the set of indices
J
as the union of the set of primes with
\{<\}
. For every
j\in J
, we have natural restriction maps
S^{m}_{\textup{qf}}(A)\longrightarrow S^{m}_{j}(A)
