, it is really easy to understand forking, because one can reduce the problem to forking in dimension one. We showed in the previous section that forking in ROAG is essentially a dimension one phenomenon, so it makes sense to ask whether
\underset{}{\downfree^{\mathbf{seq}}}=\underset{}{\downfree^{\mathbf{f}}}
in these structures. We show that the answer is no with a counterexample in DOAG. The only other explicit example we know of
\underset{}{\downfree^{\mathbf{f}}}\neq\underset{}{\downfree^{\mathbf{seq}}}
is in (
[
6
]
, example 13.6), in the theory of algebraically closed valued fields. This example does not occur exclusively in the value group, one needs field-theoretic tools to make it work, which is why our example is arguably less complicated.

In the reduct to the ordered group structure of an elementary extension of the real-closed field
\mathbb{R}
, let
\varepsilon>0
such that
\Delta(\varepsilon)<\Delta(1)
. Let
A=\mathbb{Q}
,
B=\mathbb{Q}+\mathbb{Q}\sqrt{2}+\mathbb{Q}\sqrt{3}
, and
c=(c_{1},c_{2})=(\sqrt{2}+\varepsilon,\sqrt{3}+\varepsilon\sqrt{2})
.

Let us show that we have
c\underset{A}{\downfree^{\mathbf{f}}}B
. We know that any two distinct real numbers have a different cut over
\mathbb{Q}=A
, so any bounded
B
-definable closed interval that does not have any point in
A
must be a singleton. As we have
\textup{dcl}(Ac)\cap\textup{dcl}(B)=A
, we have in fact
c\underset{A}{\downfree^{\mathbf{cut}}}B
. In particular, we have
c\underset{A}{\downfree^{\mathbf{f}}}B
.

Now let us show
c\underset{A}{{\not\downfree}^{\mathbf{seq}}}B
. Let
d=(d_{1},\ldots d_{n})
be a finite tuple from
\textup{acl}(Ac)
such that
\textup{acl}(Ac)\subseteq\textup{acl}(Ad)
. We can assume that the family
d
is
\mathbb{Q}
-free over
A
, for if
d_{i}\in\textup{dcl}(Ad_{<i})
, then we trivially have
d_{i}\underset{Ad_{<i}}{\downfree^{\mathbf{f}}}B
. We can also assume
d_{i}\in\mathbb{Q}c_{1}+\mathbb{Q}c_{2}
, for if
a\in A
, then
\textup{acl}(Ad_{\leqslant_{i}})=\textup{acl}(Ad_{<i}(d_{i}+a))
, so we can freely translate any
d_{i}
by an element of
A
. By basic linear algebra, we have
n=2
, and
(d_{1},d_{2})
is a
\mathbb{Q}
-basis of
\mathbb{Q}c_{1}+\mathbb{Q}c_{2}
. Let
f_{i}
be the unique map of
\textup{LC}^{2}(\mathbb{Q})
such that
f_{i}(c_{1},c_{2})=d_{i}
. As
(c_{1},c_{2})
is also
\mathbb{Q}
-free,
(f_{1},f_{2})
must be
\mathbb{Q}
-free. For any
g\in\textup{LC}^{2}(\mathbb{Q})
:

\begin{array}[]{ccl}d_{2}-g(1,d_{1})&=&f_{2}(\sqrt{2},\sqrt{3})-g(1,f_{1}(\sqrt{2},\sqrt{3}))\\
&&+\varepsilon\left[f_{2}(1,\sqrt{2})-g(0,f_{1}(1,\sqrt{2}))\right]\end{array}

As
(f_{1},f_{2})
and
(1,\sqrt{2},\sqrt{3})
are both
\mathbb{Q}
-free, we must have:

f_{2}(\sqrt{2},\sqrt{3})-g(1,f_{1}(\sqrt{2},\sqrt{3}))\neq 0

so
\Delta(d_{2}-g(1,d_{1}))=\Delta(1)
. We established that, for all
x\in\textup{dcl}(Ad_{1})
, we have
\Delta(d_{2}-x)>\Delta(\varepsilon)
, which implies
\varepsilon\in H(d_{2}/\textup{dcl}(Ad_{1}))
. Now, if we had
d_{2}\underset{Ad_{1}}{\downfree^{\mathbf{f}}}B
, then we would necessarily have
\varepsilon\in H(d_{2}/\textup{dcl}(Bd_{1}))
by
proposition

2.9
. Let us show that this fails. It is enough to show that
\Delta(d_{2}-b)=\Delta(\varepsilon)
for some
b\in\textup{dcl}(Bd_{1})
. Choose
b=d_{1}-f_{1}(\sqrt{2},\sqrt{3})+f_{2}(\sqrt{2},\sqrt{3})
. Then, as
d_{i}=f_{i}(c_{1},c_{2})=f_{i}(\sqrt{2},\sqrt{3})+f_{i}(\varepsilon,\varepsilon\sqrt{2})
, we have
d_{2}-b=f_{2}(\varepsilon,\varepsilon\sqrt{2})-f_{1}(\varepsilon,\varepsilon\sqrt{2})
. As
(f_{1},f_{2})
is
\mathbb{Q}
-free,
f_{2}-f_{1}\neq 0
, therefore
d_{2}-b
is a non-trivial linear combination of
(\varepsilon,\varepsilon\sqrt{2})
. This latter family is clearly
\Delta
-separated, thus
\Delta(d_{2}-b)=\Delta(\varepsilon)
, and we can conclude.

This holds for any choice for the family
d
, so we have
c\underset{A}{{\not\downfree}^{\mathbf{seq}}}B
, which concludes the example.

\textup{dcl}(Ac_{1})
\textup{dcl}(Ac_{1})
c_{2}
\sqrt{3}\in\textup{dcl}(Bc_{1})
\sqrt{3}+2\varepsilon\in\textup{dcl}(Bc_{1})

9.3.
Weak versus strong versions of sequential independence

In the definition of sequential independence, we require that there exists a good enumeration
d
that witnesses independence. Does it change anything to ask this for all the enumerations? We show that the answer is yes with a counterexample in DOAG.

Definition 9.2
.

Define
C\underset{A}{\downfree^{\mathbf{seq}*}}B
when, for every finite tuple
c=(c_{0}..c_{n})
from
\textup{acl}(AC)
, we have
c_{i}\underset{Ac_{<i}}{\downfree^{\mathbf{f}}}B
for all
i\leqslant n
.

This notion is at least as strong as
\underset{}{\downfree^{\mathbf{seq}}}
, which is strictly stronger than
\underset{}{\downfree^{\mathbf{f}}}
in DOAG by the above example. Let us show that
\underset{}{\downfree^{\mathbf{seq}*}}
is strictly stronger than
\underset{}{\downfree^{\mathbf{seq}}}
in DOAG.
Let
\varepsilon>0
,
c_{2}>0
such that
\Delta(\varepsilon)<\Delta(c_{2})<\Delta(1)
. Let
A=\mathbb{Q}
,
B=\mathbb{Q}+\mathbb{Q}\sqrt{2}
, and
c_{1}=\sqrt{2}+\varepsilon
.

With the same reasoning as in the previous example,
c_{1}c_{2}\underset{A}{\downfree^{\mathbf{cut}}}B
. In particular, we have
c_{1}\underset{A}{\downfree^{\mathbf{f}}}B
and
c_{2}\underset{A}{\downfree^{\mathbf{f}}}B
.

As
\Delta(c_{2})\not\in\Delta(\textup{dcl}(Ac_{1}))
,
c_{2}
is ramified over
\textup{dcl}(Ac_{1})
. The only points of
\textup{dcl}(Bc_{1})
that have the same cut over
\textup{dcl}(Ac_{1})
as that of
c_{2}
are the
\lambda\varepsilon
, with
\lambda\in\mathbb{Q}_{>0}
. They are all smaller than
c_{2}
, so
c_{2}
leans right with respect to
\textup{dcl}(Ac_{1})
and
\textup{dcl}(Bc_{1})
, and
c_{2}\underset{Ac_{1}}{\downfree^{\mathbf{f}}}B
, which implies
c_{1}c_{2}\underset{A}{\downfree^{\mathbf{seq}}}B
.

Let us show
c_{1}c_{2}\underset{A}{{\not\downfree}^{\mathbf{seq}*}}B
. The interval
[\sqrt{2},\sqrt{2}+c_{2}]
contains
c_{1}
and has no point in
\textup{dcl}(Ac_{2})
, which implies
c_{1}\underset{Ac_{2}}{{\not\downfree}^{\mathbf{f}}}B
.

9.4.
Forking versus geometric cut-dependence

Let us look at a different definition for cut independence:

Definition 9.3
.

Let
{M}
be the expansion of some totally ordered set, and
A
,
B
,
C
parameter sets. We write
C\underset{A}{{\not\downfree}^{\mathbf{cut}*}}B
when there exists
c\in\textup{dcl}(AC)
and
b_{1}
,
b_{2}\in\textup{dcl}(AB)
such that
b_{1}\leqslant c\leqslant b_{2}
,
b_{1}\equiv_{A}b_{2}
and
b_{i}\not\in\textup{dcl}(A)
.

We clearly have the implications
\underset{}{\downfree^{\mathbf{d}}}\subseteq\underset{}{\downfree^{\mathbf{cut}*}}
and:

C\underset{A}{\downfree^{\mathbf{cut}*}}B\Longleftarrow\textup{div}(\textup{dcl}(AC))\underset{\textup{div}(\textup{dcl}(A))}{\downfree^{\mathbf{cut}}}\textup{div}(\textup{dcl}(AB))

in any ordered Abelian group. It is not hard to prove that these two implications are equivalences in DOAG. In a dense model of ROAG
M
, if
B
is an
|A|^{+}
-saturated model containing
A
, then one may show that the second implication is an equivalence, i.e.
C\underset{A}{\downfree^{\mathbf{cut}*}}B
holds in
{M}
if and only if
\textup{div}(C)\underset{\textup{div}(A)}{\downfree^{\mathbf{cut}}}\textup{div}(B)
holds in
\textup{div}(M)
.

Whether the first inclusion is an equality is a little more complicated. Let
G
be an ordered Abelian group,
A
a parameter set, and
B
an
|A|^{+}
-saturated model containing
A
. In case
G
is regular and
|{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.41666pt}{$\displaystyle{G}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.47221pt}{$\displaystyle{lG}$}}{\raisebox{3.41666pt}{$\textstyle{G}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.47221pt}{$\textstyle{lG}$}}{\raisebox{2.39166pt}{$\scriptstyle{G}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.43054pt}{$\scriptstyle{lG}$}}{\raisebox{1.70833pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{G}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.7361pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{lG}$}}}|
is infinite for some prime
l
(this is possible, take for instance
G=\sum\limits_{n<\omega}\mathbb{Z}r_{n}
, with
(r_{n})_{n}
a
\mathbb{Q}
-free subfamily of
\mathbb{R}
), the characterization of forking and dividing that we have in
theorem

8.1
implies that
\underset{A}{\downfree^{\mathbf{d}}}B
does not coincide with
\underset{A}{\downfree^{\mathbf{cut}*}}B
, because we have partial types
\textup{ mod }\equiv_{l}
that divide over
A
. However, if
G
is dp-minimal, then we have indeed
\underset{A}{\downfree^{\mathbf{f}}}B=\underset{A}{\downfree^{\mathbf{cut}*}}B
. In fact, Simon showed in (
[
11
]
, Proposition 2.5) that, for
c
a singleton, we have
c\underset{A}{\downfree^{\mathbf{f}}}B
if and only if
c\underset{A}{\downfree^{\mathbf{cut}*}}B
if
G
is dp-minimal, even when
G
is not regular. However, we will see an example where
G
is a dp-minimal, poly-regular, non-regular dense ordered Abelian group where
c\underset{A}{\downfree^{\mathbf{cut}*}}B
(and thus
c\underset{A}{\downfree^{\mathbf{f}}}B
) holds in
G
, but
c\underset{\textup{div}(A)}{\downfree^{\mathbf{cut}}}\textup{div}(B)
does not hold in
\textup{div}(G)
.

Let
G
be any ordered group elementarily equivalent to the lexicographical product
\mathbb{Z}\left[\dfrac{1}{2}\right]\times\mathbb{Z}\left[\dfrac{1}{2}\right]
. Then
G
is clearly dp-minimal, poly-regular and dense. In
\mathbb{Z}\left[\dfrac{1}{2}\right]\times\mathbb{Z}\left[\dfrac{1}{2}\right]
, we have
H_{3}((1,0))=\{0\}\times\mathbb{Z}\left[\dfrac{1}{2}\right]\neq\{0\}
, so
G
is not regular, and it has exactly one proper non-trivial definable convex subgroup, let us call it
H(G)
. Let
A
be a definably closed parameter subset of
G
,
B
an
|A|^{+}
-saturated elementary extension of
G
, and
{M}
a
|B|^{+}
-saturated elementary extension of
B
. Let
c\in H({M})
such that
c>H(B)
. Then we clearly have
c\underset{A}{\downfree^{\mathbf{cut}*}}B
, hence
c\underset{A}{\downfree^{\mathbf{f}}}B
by
[
11
]
. Let us show
c\underset{\textup{div}(A)}{{\not\downfree}^{\mathbf{cut}}}\textup{div}(B)
in
\textup{div}(M)
(in fact, we even show
c\underset{\textup{div}(G)}{{\not\downfree}^{\mathbf{cut}}}\textup{div}(B)
). Let
b_{1}\in H(B)
such that
b_{1}>H(G)
. Let
b_{2}\in B
such that
b_{2}\not\in H(B)
, but
b_{2}<g
for every
g\in G_{>0}\setminus H(G)
. Then the interval
I=[b_{1},b_{2}]
contains
c
.

H(G)
H(B)
G\setminus H(G)
B\setminus H(B)
c
b_{1}
b_{2}

Let us show that
I
is disjoint from
\textup{div}(G)
. Let
g\in\textup{div}(G)
with
0<g<b_{2}
, and suppose by contradiction
g>b_{1}
. Let
0<N<\omega
such that
Ng\in G
. We have
Ng\geqslant g>b_{1}
, so
Ng\not\in H(G)
. The ordered group
{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.41666pt}{$\displaystyle{G}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.41666pt}{$\displaystyle{HG}$}}{\raisebox{3.41666pt}{$\textstyle{G}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.41666pt}{$\textstyle{HG}$}}{\raisebox{2.39166pt}{$\scriptstyle{G}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.39166pt}{$\scriptstyle{HG}$}}{\raisebox{1.70833pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{G}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.70833pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{HG}$}}}
is dense (as
\mathbb{Z}\left[\dfrac{1}{2}\right]
is), so there exists
g^{\prime}\in G
such that we have
g^{\prime}\not\in H(G)
and
Ng^{\prime}<(Ng\textup{ mod }H(G))
(choose
N
distinct cosets between
H(G)
and
(Ng\textup{ mod }H(G))
, choose
g^{\prime}
a representative of the coset that corresponds to the minimal distance between them in
{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.41666pt}{$\displaystyle{G}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{H(G)}$}}{\raisebox{3.41666pt}{$\textstyle{G}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\textstyle{H(G)}$}}{\raisebox{2.39166pt}{$\scriptstyle{G}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.625pt}{$\scriptstyle{H(G)}$}}{\raisebox{1.70833pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{G}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{H(G)}$}}}
). As
g^{\prime}\not\in H(G)
and
g^{\prime}>0
, we have
b_{2}<g^{\prime}
. However, we have
Ng^{\prime}<Ng<Nb_{2}
, a contradiction. This concludes the example.

For future work, we should try to generalize our results to dp-minimal ordered groups. In such a group, one can hope that a global type over a monster model
B
does not fork over
A
if and only if its realizations
C
satisfy
C\underset{A}{\downfree^{\mathbf{cut}*}}B
. The dimension one case has already been proved by Simon.

9.5.
Forking versus Presburger-quantifier-free dependence

For this last example, we study an ordered Abelian group
G
with definably closed parameter sets
A\subseteq B
, and a singleton
c\in G
for which
c\underset{\textup{div}(A)}{\downfree^{\mathbf{cut}}}\textup{div}(B)
in
\textup{div}(G)
(hence
c\underset{A}{\downfree^{\mathbf{cut}*}}B
), and:

\left(\textup{dcl}(Ac)+l^{N}G\right)\cap\left(B+l^{N}G\right)=A+l^{N}G

for every prime
l
,
N>0
, but
c\underset{A}{{\not\downfree}^{\mathbf{d}}}B
. The dividing definable set will not be built from the cuts or the cosets
\textup{ mod }l
, but from the chain of definable convex subgroups of
G
. Our example needs to have an infinite spine.

Let
\Gamma
be the lexicographical product
\mathbb{Q}_{\leqslant 0}\times\mathbb{Z}_{\leqslant 0}
. Let
G
be the ordered group of all Hahn series with coefficients in
\mathbb{Z}
and powers in
\Gamma
(the valuation coincides with the Archimedean valuation). Then
G
is a dense ordered Abelian group. For each
g\in G
, let
S(g)\subseteq\Gamma
be the support of
g
. Each
g\in G
can be written
\sum\limits_{\delta\in S(g)}\lambda_{\delta}t^{\delta}
, with
\lambda_{\delta}\in\mathbb{Z}
, and
t
an indeterminate. If
\sigma
is an automorphism of the ordered set
\mathbb{Q}_{\leqslant 0}
, then let
\bar{\sigma}\in\textup{Aut}(G)
be defined as
\sum\limits_{(\mu,n)\in\Gamma}\lambda_{(\mu,n)}t^{(\mu,n)}\longmapsto\sum\limits_{(\mu,n)\in\Gamma}\lambda_{(\mu,n)}t^{(\sigma(\mu),n)}
. Let
A=\left\{a\in G|S(a)\subseteq\left(\{0\}\times\mathbb{Z}_{\leqslant 0}\right)\right\}
and
B=\textup{dcl}\left(A\cup\{t^{(-1,0)}\}\right)
. The action on
\mathbb{Q}_{\leqslant 0}
of its automorphism group has only two orbits:
\{0\}
and
\mathbb{Q}_{<0}
. As a result, we must have
\textup{dcl}(\emptyset)\subseteq A
. By (
[
2
]
, Corollary 1.10),
\textup{dcl}(A)
is exactly the relative divisible closure in
G
of
A
, in other words
A
is definably closed. With the same argument, we have
B=A+\mathbb{Z}t^{(-1,0)}
. Let
c=t^{(-1,-1)}
, so
\textup{dcl}(Ac)=A+\mathbb{Z}c
. We have
\Delta(c)<\Delta(B_{\neq 0})
, so
c
clearly leans left with respect to
\textup{div}(A)
and
\textup{div}(B)
in
\textup{div}(G)
, which implies
c\underset{A}{\downfree^{\mathbf{cut}}}B
in
\textup{div}(G)
. Moreover, for every prime
l
, and
N>0
, the elements of
l^{N}G
are exactly the series
\sum\limits_{\delta}l^{N}\lambda_{\delta}t^{\delta}
with
\lambda_{\delta}\in\mathbb{Z}
, so we clearly have
\left(\textup{dcl}(Ac)+l^{N}G\right)\cap\left(B+l^{N}G\right)=A+l^{N}G
.

A
B
0
c

Now let us prove
c\underset{A}{{\not\downfree}^{\mathbf{d}}}B
. For each
n<\omega
, let
\sigma_{n}\in\textup{Aut}(\mathbb{Q}_{\leqslant 0})
such that
\sigma_{n}(1)=-n
. For each prime
l
, for each
g=\sum\limits_{\delta}\lambda_{\delta}t^{\delta}\in G
, if
g\not\in l^{N}G
, then one can show (see for instance
[
2
]
, example 4.2) that
H_{l}^{N}(g)
is the group of elements of
G
with an Archimedean class smaller or equal to
\Delta(t^{\delta})
, where
\delta
is the largest element of
S(g)
for which
\lambda_{\delta}\not\in l^{N}\mathbb{Z}
. As a result, there does not exist
g\in G
for which
H_{2}(c)<H_{2}(g)<H_{2}(t^{(-1,0)})
. The following
B
-definable set which contains
c
:

X=\left\{g\in G|g\not\in 2G\wedge\left(\forall h\in G,\ \left(H_{2}(h)\leqslant H_{2}(g)\vee H_{2}(t^{(-1,0)})\leqslant H_{2}(h)\right)\right)\right\}

divides over
A
, because the
(\bar{\sigma_{n}}(X))_{n}
are pairwise-disjoint. This concludes the example.
